- Freelancer type: Independent. [L1430.013]
- Job success score: set the filter to 90% or higher and reject any candidate below 90%. Freelancers who take on too much work tend to miss deadlines and see their score drop. [L1430.014]
- Minimum experience on Upwork: 100 hours, which shows the candidate has already done some work on the platform. [L1430.015]
- Location: does not matter for an email, live chat and order processing role. [L1430.016]
- English level: set to Fluent. Do not require Native or Bilingual, since the Fluent filter already includes these candidates. [L1430.016]
- Expect applications within the first 30 minutes after posting, and many of them. Plan time to review them quickly. [L1430.018]

#### Keyword and phrase instructions in the post

- Include a keyword filter in the job title or description, such as: "To apply successfully, include [specific phrase] in your application" (for example, "include your best color"). This filters out low-effort applicants automatically. [L0263.010]
- Ask applicants to put a specific phrase, for example "customer service is my favorite", in the subject line of their application. [L0337.022]
- Replace keyword filtering with an Asana form submission, which is the modern approach. Direct applicants to complete the Asana form to apply successfully. Form responses become Asana tasks with the candidate's email as the task name. Customize 6 key screening questions in the form. [L0263.012]

#### Template for a VA job post

The template text is in the source as follows. Keep it unchanged.

```text
I'd like to bring on a Virtual Assistant to help run my dropshipping business. Here are the tasks I'm planning to hand off:
1. Handling customer inquiries and support
2. Managing order fulfillment
3. Building out the online store

Drop me a message if this sounds like something you'd want to take on!
```
[L0765.008]

#### Template for content writers

The source gives no template text for this role. Build your own by listing: [L0379.006]
- the deliverable,
- the required elements of each piece,
- the pay per project,
- the test project.

When hiring content writers, list three requirements in the job post: time management, customer service experience and writing experience. Check each applicant against all three before offering a paid trial task. Hire separately for other roles rather than expecting one person to cover everything. [L1396.068]

#### Tasks that need market-based staff

- Tasks that involve calling suppliers in a market are best given to someone based in that market. Matching time zone, business hours and accent makes suppliers more comfortable. The post should require that location and tell people not to apply if they are elsewhere. [L1055.007]

#### Delegation posts on Upwork

- When you need to free up time and delegate, post a job on Upwork that describes the exact task you want someone else to do. Applications then arrive from freelancers who want that work. [L1418.001]
- Use Upwork with a detailed job post and screening questions when you need a VA for ongoing work. Use Fiverr for small, one-off tasks to test how a VA works before committing to a longer hire. [L1418.012]

### Pay-rate disclosure

- Publish a starting pay rate in the post. It filters out candidates who would reject the pay anyway, so you interview only people already within budget. Candidates should see whether the figure is in their range before applying. [L1055.005]
- The pay rate is a required item in the post. [L1000.026]
- Pay type on Upwork: choose hourly when first testing someone, and fixed price once a good candidate is found. [L1430.010]
- Fixed-batch example: a post for a product upload VA offered a fixed price of 200 for 179 products, with descriptions, images and prices supplied by the store owner. A fixed batch with a clear deliverable makes it easy to compare applicants and test them cheaply. [L0832.061]
- Freelancers on Upwork with no ratings yet can often be hired at lower rates if you vet them yourself by reading their work samples. The extra vetting time is the trade-off for the lower price. [L0382.053]
- VAs on Upwork are priced very differently, so compare rates and profiles before hiring. [L0829.024]
- You can state whether you will train a less experienced person at a lower rate. [L1000.026]
- Well-paid and well-treated VAs stay loyal. [L1437.027]
- Overqualified hires cost more per hour but need fewer explanations, make their own decisions, and are less likely to leave for a better salary. [L1473.019]

### Location and time zone

- Location only needs to be stated on platforms that are not location-limited. On OnlineJobs.ph all candidates are based in the Philippines. On platforms such as FreeUp or Upwork, a required location (for example, the US) must be written into the post. [L1055.006]
- Time zone matters, but it is not decisive. A location close to the target market's time zone makes working hours easier to share. For example, the Philippines is three to four hours from Australia, depending on daylight saving. [L1000.045]
- Factor a VA's time zone and location into the hire. A VA in the Philippines works different hours than one in the US, which affects when calls and questions can be answered. [L0832.064]
- Regions with a similar time zone can reduce friction for managers. Some owners use Morocco on Upwork for this reason. [L1077.034]

### Application filters and screening tricks

#### Filters that run before any interview

- Simple instructions for how to submit the application and what it must contain are the first screening layer. They show whether a candidate reads and follows directions. [L1055.008]
- Candidates who ignore the submission format or required content should not be interviewed. Ignoring simple instructions before hiring predicts ignoring SOPs after hiring. [L1055.009]
- Ask a screening question in the Upwork application, such as a simple arithmetic question like 2+2. Applicants who do not answer it have likely not read the job description and can be ignored. [L1418.002]
- A correct answer to a screening question shows that an applicant read the full job description and is serious about the work. Skipping the screening question is a sign of random, mass applications. [L1418.003]
- A simple screening question, such as a basic arithmetic question, shows whether an applicant read the full job description. Applicants who skip screening questions are usually applying at random and can be ignored. [L1433.004]
- Filter out everyone who did not answer the screening questions. [L1433.003]
- Hidden secret word: add a random secret word, for example "tuna fish", to the post with a request to include it in the reply email. Discard applicants who omit the word, since they most likely did not read the post. [L0921.015]
- Secret-word filter result: it reduced hundreds of VA applications to three qualified candidates. The remaining candidates were built into a long-term working relationship. [L0921.016]
- Easter egg: ask applicants to add a small picture of a purple giraffe at the top of their application, and reject those who miss it. Most applicants miss this instruction, so it separates careful candidates from others. [L1441.027]
- Current employment question: "Do you currently hold a similar position in another company? If yes, what type of clients/products are you responsible for?" This is the most selective question. Candidates answering "yes" should not advance. You need people fully committed to your company, not part-timers juggling multiple employers. [L0263.013]

#### Screening tests after the first filter

Criteria every applicant must meet, checked against each applicant: [L0926.008]
1. Internet speed screenshot.
2. Audio recording of the applicant's voice sample.
3. English grammar test.

Screening steps in order: [L1441.027]
1. Require a passing score of 80 percent or higher on an English grammar test.
2. Ask for a screenshot of an internet speed test showing upload and download speeds.
3. Request an audio recording of the candidate reading a short text aloud, to check that their English is good enough for customer calls.
4. Include an easter egg in the job posting (see the purple giraffe filter above).

#### Video and audio answers

- Ask a few questions that require a short video or audio answer, for example "why do you want this job". [L0337.022]
- Discard written answers that you would not be willing to send to a customer. Keep those that show the person can talk to customers well. [L0337.022]
- For ongoing hires, interview the candidate by video using prepared questions, as you would for an employee. [L1122.018]

#### Experience requirements

Sources differ on how much prior experience to require. Each applies in a different situation.

- Experience solving problems matters more than Shopify experience for US roles, because small details can be learned on the job. Hire experienced freelancers for US roles through Upwork and for Philippines-based roles through OnlineJobs.ph. [L0337.021]
- For most Shopify high-ticket dropshipping tasks, experienced people already exist, so requiring proof of prior experience in the application is realistic. Candidates without that experience can be filtered out without losing good options. [L1055.036]
- Hiring someone overqualified, with good English and years of customer service experience, saves time. They need fewer explanations, make their own decisions, and are less likely to leave for a better salary. [L1473.019]
- For a VA who answers emails, handles live chat and processes orders, Upwork's entry-level setting is the correct experience level. This is a separate setting from how overqualified you are willing to hire. [L1430.011]
- Write a moderate job description and prefer less experienced candidates when you will train them to your own processes. (older, 2021 source) [L1437.024]

#### Language

- The working language must be the language the business and its customers use, whatever that language is. A candidate whose written language is weak can be rejected even if the weakness comes from a non-native background, because a writing standard cannot be taught on the job. [L1055.035]
- Frequent misspellings or poor grammar are a reason to remove a candidate, since this cannot be taught later. [L1055.011]

### Shortlisting rules

#### Funnel numbers

Sources give different numbers. Use the one that matches your post volume.

| Stage | Example from source | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Applications to a popular post | Around 50, or hundreds | L1055.010 |
| Applications to a large post | About 180 | L1077.034 |
| Applications to a hiring funnel example | About 400 total | L0263.045 |
| Pass keyword or form filter | About 200 → 50-60 | L0263.010, L0263.045 |
| Final interview candidates | 5-6 | L0263.045 |
| Interviewed | Only 3 to 5 | L1055.010 |
| Hired | 1 | L0263.045 |

Note the difference: L0263.045 has 5-6 final interview candidates, while L1055.010 says only 3 to 5 should be interviewed. Keep the shortlist to 3 to 5 if your team can handle that size. [L0263.045, L1055.010]

Other figures:
- Expect hundreds of applicants, so shortlist, test several candidates at the same time, and compare results before choosing. [L0467.069]
- Expect hundreds of applications to a job post, and judge candidates by past work samples. [L0971.006]
- Filtering early saves interview time. [L0263.045]

#### Shortlisting order

Shortlist applicants in this order: [L1055.011]
1. Check whether the application followed the posted instructions. If not, delete it without reply.
2. Check whether the candidate has experience with the task. If the posting asked for proof of experience and it is missing, remove the candidate. People who have never done the task are generally filtered out.
3. Check attention to detail: effort in the application, full written answers versus one-liners, quality of any CV or resume, spelling and sentence structure. Drop candidates who did the bare minimum.
4. Check command of the working language, which must be good for the customer, product and content work involved. Frequent misspellings or poor grammar are a reason to remove the candidate, since this cannot be taught later.

#### Working the shortlist

- Shortlist applicants in a spreadsheet, such as Google Sheets, before sending follow-up questions. The follow-up can repeat the job post questions or be new ones. [L1000.028]
- Shortlist the applications you like in a spreadsheet, then email follow-up questions to the shortlisted candidates. [L1000.025]
- Reply only to the candidates you shortlist, because replying to hundreds of applicants is too time consuming. If you have an executive assistant, that person can handle the replies to the others. [L1055.012]
- If more candidates remain after the four checks than the interview limit allows, rank the remaining candidates and keep the top five. If the candidates still cannot be separated, choose randomly and move on without overthinking. [L1055.013]

#### Follow-up questions

- Review the follow-up answers for responsiveness, spelling and grammar, and whether the answers look scripted. Drop candidates who take days to reply. [L1000.025]
- Send the remaining applicants a follow-up with specific questions about their knowledge and experience for the job. [L1396.049]

### Interviews, trials and hiring

#### Interview and trial

- Run a five-minute interview test on a call with the best candidates. Offer a half-day paid trial to the last three or four. [L1000.025]
- Test with scenario questions and a small paid trial batch, such as three or four product descriptions with minimal instructions, to see how they show initiative. [L1122.018]
- Give a paid trial task and measure how fast it is done. [L1396.049]
- Give a trial task before a long-term commitment. [L0832.044]
- Before offering a paid trial task, check each content applicant against all three requirements. [L1396.068]

#### Checks before hiring

- Check reviews on the platform, especially recurring themes that need discussion. [L1122.018]
- Check portfolios and ask previous clients for references. [L1122.018]
- Before hiring a freelancer on Fiverr, check the number of reviews and the feedback left by previous clients. Reviews serve as social proof of reliability. [L1433.006, L1418.005]
- Adding a line at the end of an Upwork job post that points to your positive client reviews can help attract more applicants. This works only if you already have good client feedback on the platform. [L0382.054]
- A Klaviyo specialist can be found on Upwork or similar sites. Ask for screenshots or a recording of past flows before hiring. [L0855.169]
- To find a Shopify bookkeeper, search Upwork for Shopify bookkeeping and review the profiles with direct e-commerce experience. [L0855.167]
- Define a list of criteria that every applicant must meet, then check each applicant against it. [L0926.008]

#### Five-step VA hiring process (version A)

1. Write a very detailed job post. [L1000.025]
2. Shortlist the applications you like in a spreadsheet, then email follow-up questions to the shortlisted candidates. [L1000.025]
3. Review the follow-up answers for responsiveness, spelling and grammar, and whether the answers look scripted. Drop candidates who take days to reply. [L1000.025]
4. Run a five-minute interview test on a call with the best candidates, and offer a half-day paid trial to the last three or four. [L1000.025]
5. Sign an agreement letter, then train the new hire gradually, starting with email and moving to phone calls and live chat. [L1000.025]

#### Six-step freelancer and VA sequence (version B)

1. Write a clear, attractive job posting that states the job, expectations, pay, and weekly hours, so good candidates are attracted and weak ones are filtered out. [L1122.018]
2. Check reviews on the platform, especially recurring themes that need discussion. [L1122.018]
3. Ask your business community or forums for recommendations. [L1122.018]
4. For ongoing hires, interview the candidate by video using prepared questions, as you would for an employee. [L1122.018]
5. Test with scenario questions and a small paid trial batch, such as three or four product descriptions with minimal instructions, to see how they show initiative. [L1122.018]
6. Check portfolios and ask previous clients for references. [L1122.018]

#### Five-step paid trial approach (version C)

1. Post a job with a clear list of required skills and tasks. [L1396.049]
2. Ignore applicants who do not read the requirements, such as those who send only a generic message or a copied resume. [L1396.049]
3. Send the remaining applicants a follow-up with specific questions about their knowledge and experience for the job. [L1396.049]
4. Give a paid trial task and measure how fast it is done. [L1396.049]
5. Keep the people who deliver quality and convert the best part-timers to full-time. [L1396.049]

#### Small first task

Procedure for a first VA hire: [L0832.044]
1. Post a job ad for a small, clearly defined task, such as uploading a batch of products with descriptions, images and prices.
2. Review the candidates' profiles and give a trial task before a long-term commitment.
3. Keep the first task simple so a bad result is easy to correct by deleting and rehiring.

#### Fiverr procedures

To find a VA on Fiverr: [L1418.006]
1. Type "virtual assistant" into the Fiverr search bar.
2. Compare the sellers on offer and check how many reviews each one has.
3. Contact the sellers you consider directly.
4. Give them a task and see whether they can deliver it.

To find a VA on Fiverr for small tasks: [L1433.011]
1. Type "virtual assistant" into the Fiverr search bar.
2. Look for sellers who have a good number of reviews.
3. Contact the sellers directly and give each one a concrete small task to see whether they can deliver.
4. Pay only after you accept the finished job, so the seller is paid only if you are satisfied.

Fiverr suits small tasks and small store projects. Use it to test how a VA works before committing to a larger role. [L1418.007, L1433.007]

#### Upwork procedure

Hiring a VA on Upwork: [L1433.003]
1. Post a job with the exact tasks you want to delegate.
2. Write a clear job description that states those tasks.
3. Add screening questions to the job application.
4. Review applicants and filter out everyone who did not answer the screening questions.

#### Agency intake (Monazar VAs)

To request a VA through Monazar VAs: [L1418.009]
1. Open the Monazar VAs website.
2. Scroll down to the contact form.
3. Enter your name, your email address, and either your Skype ID or your WhatsApp number.
4. Write a brief explanation of what you need and how a VA should help your business.
5. Submit the form and wait for the team to contact you and set up a consultation call to discuss your needs.

Typical intake steps with an agency-based VA service: [L1433.009]
1. Open the provider's website and fill in the contact form with your name, email address, and either your Skype ID or WhatsApp number.
2. Briefly describe what you need and how a VA could help you.
3. Expect a call from the provider to run a needs analysis, where they confirm your requirements and whether they can help.

#### Hiring for the business's own processes

- Recruit VAs through platforms such as OnlineJobs.ph and Upwork. Use the Empire Flippers guide as a reference. [L1441.026]
- Check your own requirements before the first hire. [L1055.004]
- Hire a VA with experience solving problems rather than Shopify experience alone, when the role is in the US. [L0337.021]

#### Further details

- Action for a business starting content marketing without any writer: post a job for the first content creator on ProBlogger today using the template, since finding the right person takes time before content production can start. [L0379.016]

- To find English-speaking virtual assistants in the Philippines, use freelance marketplaces such as Upwork or onlinejobs.ph, where you can filter candidates yourself. Agencies that pre-screen English skills exist but are an alternative. [L0829.025]

- OnlineJobs.ph is a hiring source for workers in the Philippines and offers a service for finding them. Check its service options before hiring from the platform. [L0832.043]

- Upwork and Onlinejobs.ph are freelance platforms used to hire virtual assistants (VAs) for a remote business. [L0926.007]

## Interviewing, paid trials and selecting hires

### Screening the applicant pool

#### Filtering on application answers and written instructions

1. Ask each applicant whether they have done a similar customer service role before. If yes, ask for a link to a company they worked for. Ask for examples of typical email correspondence they handled, to judge how they communicate. Decide whether they are the right hire based on these answers. [L1430.017]
2. Use the follow-up answers to judge spelling, grammar, and whether the reply is a copied script. A candidate who will handle customer emails must write accurately and professionally. [L1000.029]
3. Treat slow replies to follow-up questions as a sign of low interest, because a candidate who takes a week to answer is likely applying to many jobs. [L1000.030]
4. Eliminate unsuitable candidates by providing specific written instructions and requesting a test task that mirrors actual work. [L0230.014]
5. Add a simple test question to the job listing, such as "What's 2+2?", to check whether applicants actually read the listing. [L0765.011]
6. Set the pass mark of any screening test from your own data: score a few hundred applicants from each country you hire from, then choose the cutoff based on those averages. A test you write yourself can be faked, so treat a candidate who skips it as the strongest reason to reject them. [L1359.042]

#### Full screening sequence from one source (Source A)

The source describes these steps in order. About 50 percent of applicants eliminated themselves at step 1, and roughly another 30 percent were removed at step 2.

1. Write a job post with specific application instructions, such as how to apply, and remove applicants who do not follow them; about 50 percent of applicants eliminated themselves this way.
2. Ask screening questions about English level; this removed roughly another 30 percent of applicants.
3. Send the remaining candidates a test task with three written questions: a reply to a very angry customer, a reply to a bad review, and an action plan for an unshipped order that was the company's fault.
4. Choose the best candidates and send them a scheduling link (Calendly, free) for a 15-minute video call via Skype.
5. Spread the interviews over two or three days, about two to three hours in total.
6. Record every candidate's results in a Google spreadsheet immediately after each call, so the best can be compared later.
7. Sign the contract before training starts. [L1473.020]

#### Setting the right candidate profile

- Hire for talent and problem-solving ability, not for the lowest hourly cost. A cheap hire who does not know the products forces the owner to answer the unusual customer questions personally. [L0337.011]
- Hire for qualifications rather than for the lowest cost, because weak candidates need more training than they are worth. A junior hire who needed too much training was dropped after the first month. [L0832.046]
- Hire people who are overqualified or already run their own business, because they add skills and can later run other team members. Being replaced by a strong hire is less of a threat than having weak support. [L0832.047]
- Freight and logistics problems will recur in a store that ships freight, so a team member who can call freight companies, vendors and customers and resolve the issue is essential. Look for this problem-solving skill when hiring, since product knowledge and Shopify experience can be taught. [L0337.020]
- Consider personality fit when hiring, since owners and assistants with very different styles can clash. A free 16 Personalities test taken by both parties, with the results compared, can show strengths and weaknesses to manage. [L1000.024]
- Disallow team members from working for other online companies while employed by you. This full-time commitment rule must be screened during hiring and enforced consistently to protect your knowledge, systems, and competitive advantage. [L0263.005]
- When screening virtual assistants, give work history the most weight. Favour candidates who worked for large, established companies, often in the US or Australia, and find out why they left, since a job at a small online store is a weaker signal. *(Older advice, 2019.)* [L0356.059]
- Recruit virtual assistants with the same care as a local employee, with a longer screening process and more checks. In one year using this approach, about 8 assistants were hired and only 1 was let go. *(Older advice, 2019.)* [L0356.060]

#### Using the Source C customer success hiring procedure

This is a reference procedure from the source. Interview rounds and pay differ between units; both values are shown in the section on interview formats below.

1. Start with a Google Form screening on dropshipping, e-commerce, and tools such as Zoho, Zendesk or email tools.
2. Run a multi-round interview, six rounds in the source.
3. Hire via Zoom and offer a salary of about $250 per month.
4. Onboard with Loom recordings about the brand and systems.
5. Spend week one on shadow testing, then take over all tasks from week two. [L0637.045]

### Interview formats

#### Choosing a format: typed chat or video call

Sources disagree on the medium. Use the format that fits the role:

| Format | Source | When to use it |
|---|---|---|
| Typed chat on Skype or Slack, one question at a time | Source B, 2021-2022 (older) | Hiring a VA or freelancer for mostly written, message-based work; you want a searchable written record |
| 15-minute video call via Skype, after a written test | Source A | Adding a voice check to a written screening; the short call assesses fluency and fit |

- Conduct hiring interviews via message exchange using standardized template questions rather than phone or video calls, as this method is more efficient. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L0223.013]
- Interview through typed messages instead of voice or video calls. Written chat shows responsiveness and written communication skills, which matter because most day to day communication with a VA or freelancer is by message. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.014]
- Run candidate interviews by typed chat rather than recorded voice or video, because the written record is quick to search. Finding a specific answer in an audio or video recording is much slower. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.032]
- Schedule video interviews via Skype within 2-3 days of application; keep each interview to 15 minutes. Use Calendly to schedule. [L0230.015]

#### Question set and interview opening

- Prepare a set of up to 15 interview questions and use the same core set for every candidate, changing only small details to suit the role. This keeps the candidates comparable. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.016]
- Ask a set of open questions during onboarding and interviews: a strong virtual assistant asks many questions, while one who asks few will get stuck later without raising it. Asking questions during onboarding is a good sign of a future good hire. [L1000.036]

Shortlist invitation, ready to paste:

```
Hello [Candidate name],

Thank you for applying. You have been successful and shortlisted for an interview.

Can you attend an interview at [proposed time, adjusted to the candidate's time zone]? If this does not suit you, please tell me a time that works for you.

The interview will be held by typed message on [Skype or Slack]. Please send me your [Skype ID or email address for the Slack invite] so I can set it up.

Kind regards,
[Your name]
```
[L1055.018]

Interview opening message, ready to paste:

```
Hi [Candidate name], welcome to the interview. I will send you one question at a time. Please answer each question fully before I send the next one. There is no need to rush, but please reply in detail.
```
[L1055.019]

Run the typed interview in this order:

1. Send the candidate a success notice with a proposed interview time that works for both time zones.
2. State that the interview will be held by typed message on Skype or Slack and ask for the Skype ID or the email address for the Slack invite.
3. Once the candidate has joined, post the introduction in the chat and send it.
4. Send the first question and wait for the full typed answer.
5. Send the next question only after the answer arrives, and repeat until all questions are asked.
6. Thank the candidate and say when you will reply, which is typically within three days.
Keep the chat as a written record for later comparison. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.017]

#### Stage-based video interviews (Source A)

Interview stage 1 (Short Call):

1. Duration: 10-15 minutes.
2. You have already reviewed their written application answers and recording.
3. Reintroduce the company, explain what you do, what you need.
4. Ask candidate to introduce themselves.
5. Assess English fluency, communication, and fit.
6. Communicate this is one of many short calls; only best advance.
7. Follow up with email to selected finalists for stage 2. [L0263.014]

Interview stage 2 (Final Call):

1. Duration: 45-60 minutes.
2. Prepare case study materials: link to website, SOPs, FAQs, product catalog.
3. Send materials to candidate before the call so they can prepare.
4. During call, ask realistic scenarios (e.g., 'Does this product have [feature]?' or 'What is the estimated delivery time?').
5. Answer must come from the materials provided (candidate hasn't seen products before).
6. This tests problem-solving, product learning ability, and communication under pressure.
7. Select top 5-6 candidates for final interviews from approximately 400 applicants. [L0263.015]

- Conduct the final interview call yourself, even as you scale. This final call is when you decide who joins the team. You need to know the person, they need to know you, and you both commit to working together. This builds accountability and culture from the hire's first day. [L0263.016]

#### Multi-round interviews (Source C)

- Run six to seven interview rounds before hiring anyone, to test the candidate's eagerness to learn. Starting pay for candidates who passed was often 200 to 300 USD per month. [L1431.020]
- Hiring a virtual assistant frees up your time so you can keep growing the business or scale it. Choosing the right person matters more than hiring quickly, because a poor fit costs money and progress. [L1433.001]

Note: the Source C procedure above states six rounds and about $250 per month as salary; L1431.020 states six to seven rounds and 200 to 300 USD per month as starting pay. Use the range that matches your budget and the round count you can staff.

### Practical tests

#### Phone and site-navigation test

Interview line to add phone duties to a virtual assistant role:

```
By the way, we also get about one or two calls a day. Would you be able to answer those as they come in, in addition to the email work?
```
[L0910.048]

Testing a phone-answering VA candidate:

1. Ask the candidate for a phone number and say you will call later in the week.
2. Call at random times Monday to Friday between 8 a.m. and 5 p.m. without announcing a test.
3. Count the rings before the candidate picks up.
4. Assess how professional they sound and whether they are a native English speaker.
5. Casually ask them to log into the store and open the homepage, and time how long it takes.
6. Ask them to describe the homepage and to read back the phone number shown on it.
7. Ask them to find a specific product through the search bar or the menu.
8. Ask them to read the note from the seller on that product.
9. Ask them to find a second product and tell you how long it takes to ship, and time the search.
The test shows availability, professionalism, computer readiness and store knowledge in one short call. [L0910.049]

- Testing a candidate by phone and site navigation reveals more than a CV or interview, because it shows real availability and skills. Those skills are the same ones that customers will need during a real call. [L0910.050]

#### Product uploader sample (Source A, 2024)

1. Post a job or search for candidates with relevant experience.
2. Review the work samples they have sent before.
3. Give a small sample project of 10 to 30 products.
4. Triple-check the sample work for correctness.
5. Only then give the full project. [L0971.007]

#### Test projects for freelancers

- Start every new freelancer with a test project, such as writing part of an article, before committing to multiple pieces. Use test projects to confirm quality and turnaround time, and to choose between several candidates by comparing their results. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L0379.004]
- Before hiring a virtual assistant for a task, test whether they can complete it on their own without constant checking. If you cannot trust them to work independently, they are not the right fit for the role. [L0832.058]

### Paid trials

#### Trial offer and pay

- Offer a paid half-day trial to the last three or four candidates, and walk them through the processes as if they were already hired. The trial reveals how they actually work, which a phone call cannot show. [L1000.033]
- An owner with two strong candidates on paper could not choose between them after interviews. A paid half-day trial made one candidate's superior performance clear, and that candidate was hired. [L1000.034]
- A paid half-day trial carries a small risk that a candidate who sees the business processes will start a competing store. The owner judged this risk highly unlikely and accepted it, because real work on the trial shows how a candidate performs better than an interview. [L1000.076]
- Do not hire the successful candidate outright. Tell them they are successful and offer a paid trial first, and pay the trial at the full rate for the position because they do the real work. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.025]

#### Trial steps

1. Tell the successful candidate they are selected and offer a trial.
2. Provide the SOP for the task so they can do it properly.
3. Walk them through an onboarding session to confirm they understand the task.
4. Define the trial parameters, such as its length or deliverable, which depend on the task.
5. Review the result at the end of the trial and decide whether to continue. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.026]

#### Trial length and deliverable

| Role | Trial length or deliverable | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Customer service | One-week trial, with a review at the end | Source B, 2021-2022 (older) [L1055.027] |
| Content production (e.g., blog writing) | First piece of work, for example one blog post | Source B, 2021-2022 (older) [L1055.027] |
| Any role, owner-led | Paid half-day trial, walking through processes as if already hired | Source A [L1000.033] |

Trial and delivery length depend on the role. The half-day trial is the approach for the final three or four candidates in the Source A source; the one-week trial is the Source B approach for customer service. [L1055.027, L1000.033]

#### Judging the trial

- A trial does not need to be perfect, since everyone makes mistakes in a new role. Small fixable issues are acceptable because you can develop the person, but a large quality gap means the trial is failed. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.028]

Trial failure message, ready to paste:

```
Hi [Candidate name],

Thank you for your work on the trial. Here is your payment for the trial.

Unfortunately you were not successful in the trial, so we will not be offering you ongoing work. Thank you for your time, and good luck for the future.

[Your name]
```
*(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.029]

### Evaluating candidates

#### Checks after each interview

Evaluate each interview using these checks, in order:

1. Did the candidate clearly agree to the pay, the hours and any specific hours? If not, it is a red flag and the candidate is not hired.
2. How many other clients does the candidate have? A large number is a red flag for part time or full time roles, because they will not be able to give the hours they promise.
3. Did the candidate answer in detail? Short answers of a few words are a red flag, while multi-line answers are a positive sign.
4. How quickly did the candidate reply? A long delay for a simple question suggests divided attention, and slow understanding of the question is also a red flag.
5. Did the answers feel honest and genuine rather than like fluff written to please you? Trust your gut feeling here.
6. Does the candidate fit your company culture?
Any red flag on these checks means the candidate is removed. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.022]

- The interview is not used to test the task itself, since most people learn the task quickly. It is used to assess speed, attention to detail, culture fit and red flags, and the job application already gives a good indication of experience. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.020]
- Attention to detail is required for any VA or freelancer role, because those roles follow SOPs and cannot be run with loose work. High level thinking is the owner's job and does not replace attention to detail in these roles. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.021]
- A candidate who takes about 10 minutes to start replying to a single interview question is probably dividing attention with other work. Slow replies during the interview are a warning sign about future responsiveness. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.033]
- If a part-time or full-time candidate already works with around 10 other clients, they are unlikely to deliver the hours they promise, even if they say otherwise. Treat a high number of other clients as a red flag for hours. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.034]

### Reference checks and verification

- On Upwork, recent hires have sometimes claimed experience that they did not have. Verify work samples and experience before hiring. *(Older advice, 2018.)* [L1274.078]
- Ask for a link to a company the candidate has worked for in a similar role, and ask for examples of typical email correspondence they handled. [L1430.017]
- Find out why the candidate left previous large, established companies, since this shows more than the CV alone. *(Older advice, 2019.)* [L0356.059]
- Compare the sample work sent by the candidate with a small sample project before the full project is given. [L0971.007]

### Selecting the hire

#### Deciding between finalists

- If two or three candidates pass all checks, choose the one with the strongest gut feeling, the strongest relevant experience, or the best fit with your culture. Give candidates a decision date, typically around three days after the interview. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.023]
- A negative gut feeling during the interview stage rarely improves later, and you will work with the person often, sometimes daily. Move on to another applicant when the gut feeling is negative. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.024]
- The first 3-6 months reveal whether a win-win exists: for you (business value), the team (culture fit), and the employee (fair pay, growth, respect). Set this expectation during hiring so both sides know early termination is possible if it's not working. This removes surprises and keeps focus on actual fit. [L0263.048]

### Rejecting candidates and avoiding rushed hiring

- Thank unsuccessful applicants and keep good relations with them, because they can be called on for a second assistant hire later. [L1000.040]
- Rushing the hiring process, skipping steps or skipping paid trials leads to the wrong hires. You then pay for work that does not return value, and either pay someone to redo it or spend your own time fixing it. *(Older advice, 2021-2022.)* [L1055.030]
- Hiring a virtual assistant frees up your time so you can keep growing the business or scale it. Choosing the right person matters more than hiring quickly, because a poor fit costs money and progress. [L1433.001]
- Remove any candidate who fails a check in the evaluation sequence, and send the failure message to those who completed a paid trial without success. [L1055.022, L1055.029]

#### Further details

- Maintain a candidate tracking spreadsheet for all applicants, interview notes, and hiring decisions. [L0230.016]

## Onboarding and training new team members

### Onboarding principles and the owner's role

- Onboarding is the key step with new hires and virtual assistants. Show the work, set expectations, check progress, and revisit the role from time to time, because a good resume does not guarantee delivery from day one. *(Older: 2021.)* [L0801.153]
- Expect to be closely involved in the first one to two weeks of a new hire. Answer their questions, review their work and give feedback until they work the way you want. [L1431.023]
- Before delegating a recurring task, spend substantial time at the start showing the assistant exactly how you want it done. This upfront training is what later lets a short instruction, such as switching an offer to buy one get one free, be carried out without follow-up. [L1432.040]
- Prepare a new hire to succeed by setting up boards and processes before they start, so they are not overwhelmed. Tested templates and clear procedures let new staff work well from the first day. Practise the routine with the new hire for about two weeks before they work alone. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1396.056]
- Incomplete training blocks a person from starting their assigned work. Depending on how soon you need them productive, training may be urgent and a top priority. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L0265.016]

### Before the first day

#### Onboarding SOP

- Create a separate onboarding SOP for every new hire. It explains what the business does, how the website works, which brands are sold and who the customers are. Do not assume an overseas VA or freelancer understands the business model, such as high-ticket dropshipping, unless you explain it. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1339.014, L1339.016]
- The onboarding SOP can be a simple recorded video of the owner explaining what the website does and how it works, which brands are sold, and who the customers are. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1339.016]
- The onboarding SOP should explain how the business works, what the team looks like and how the new hire can contact you. Task-specific SOPs go alongside it, and the new hire's first action item is to work through them. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1054.007]
- An onboarding SOP makes a new hire feel part of a real, legitimate business. This encourages loyalty and higher-quality work, especially for remote contractors who can feel isolated. It can also include any onboarding tasks every new person must complete on joining. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1339.015]
- If an existing training course covers the task, give the new hire access to it together with the SOP. This can cut a lot of detail from your own SOP and save time. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1054.008]

#### Onboarding questionnaire

Steps for the first contact (older: 2021-22):

1. Provide your onboarding SOP and any SOPs or training related to the task. [L1054.006]
2. Send an onboarding questionnaire to collect the new hire's details. [L1054.006]
3. Once they have gone through the SOPs and/or training, hold a meeting to set expectations, get to know them better and make sure they understand the task(s). [L1054.006]

Content and format:

- Use a standard form with the same fields for everyone you hire. Collect payment details, contact details, an emergency contact, a phone number, and their account for a messaging app such as WhatsApp or Viber, which are common in the Philippines. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1054.009]
- Collect several contact channels, not just one. Natural disasters such as earthquakes, typhoons or landslides can cut power and internet and make the usual channel unavailable. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1054.010]
- Google Forms can serve as a standardised onboarding questionnaire. All responses are stored in one place, so you can look up details such as a bank account for TransferWise or a PayPal email without searching old chat messages. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1054.011]

### First day and first contact

#### Standard onboarding tasks

1. Prepare 5-7 standard onboarding tasks in Asana, such as creating an email, accessing live chat, submitting payment info and completing the profile. [L0263.018]
2. For each new hire, copy and paste these tasks into a new Asana project. [L0263.018]
3. The new employee completes them on day 1-2. [L0263.018]
4. The tasks prevent first-day confusion and make the person productive immediately. [L0263.018]
5. Without clear tasks, new hires sit idle and lose momentum. [L0263.018]

#### Onboarding call

- Hold the onboarding call on day 1 or 2. Give a brief overview of the company, provide technical training, and explain team structure and reporting. This kickstarts the relationship and ensures the person understands the context. [L0263.023]
- In the get-to-know call with a new outsourced hire, ask about their family, where they live and what they enjoy outside work. This makes the working relationship personal, builds trust and supports long-term retention. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1054.043]

#### End-of-trial meeting

- Confirm pay, working hours and communication expectations, and check the new hire is still happy with them. Then get to know them personally, answer questions about the SOPs and training, and send them back to work to see how they perform. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1054.013]

### Training plans

#### Standard VA training procedure

1. Teach company details: about us, products, and shipping and returns policy. [L0230.018]
2. Document and review all SOPs. [L0230.018]
3. Provide a workspace overview and record a video walkthrough. [L0230.018]
4. Create a shared Google Doc for training notes and questions. [L0230.018]
5. Show training videos from apps such as Asana, Shopify and Reamaze. [L0230.018]
6. Record custom training videos with free software. [L0230.018]
7. Use learning-by-doing with real tasks. [L0230.018]
8. Explain the reasoning behind each process. [L0230.018]

#### Workspace and learning-by-doing training (up to two weeks)

- Initial training for a new VA lasts up to two weeks and ends earlier if practical checkpoints are passed. During the training period, both the new VA and an existing VA training a replacement receive half pay. [L1473.022]

1. Have the new VA read about the company, its products, and its shipping and return policies. [L1473.023]
2. Teach the standard operating procedures. [L1473.023]
3. Give the new VA a login to the shared Chrome workspace. [L1473.023]
4. Provide a short video that explains what each bookmark and dashboard is for and how to use it. [L1473.023]
5. Link to vendor help-centre videos, for example from Shopify and Asana, for general tool training. [L1473.023]
6. For procedures without training material, record a screen-share video with free software (QuickTime on a Mac) and upload it to the shared drive. [L1473.023]
7. Use learning by doing: first demonstrate a task, then let the VA do it themselves. When a mistake happens, explain why it is wrong and the logic behind company policy and culture. [L1473.023]

#### Staged rollout by channel

1. Start with email only. Process each incoming email together on a call, and explain why each reply is written and who it goes to. [L1000.039]
2. Once emails run smoothly, introduce phone calls. [L1000.039]
3. After phone calls, introduce live chat and other tasks. [L1000.039]
4. Give more responsibility gradually. Do not expect to leave the business unattended right after hiring. [L1000.039]

#### Three-step procedure for a new VA (Source C)

1. Invest time at the beginning to show exactly what you expect. Keep expectations low during the first week. [L0765.012]
2. Be approachable and open to questions. Define all dos and don'ts for the business, including what authority the assistant has and which important policies must be followed. [L0765.012]
3. Control the work: set deadlines, check completed work, give feedback, and agree on a reporting rhythm (daily, weekly or monthly). [L0765.012]

#### Two-week plan (older)

*(Older: 2021-22.)* Use this plan to train a new virtual assistant:

1. Introduce the company using the corporate information board. [L1396.055]
2. Have them follow the morning routine with you every day for two weeks. [L1396.055]
3. Create one draft order yourself, then have them create one while you watch. [L1396.055]
4. Have them watch the training videos twice. [L1396.055]
5. Arrange a one-hour training call with a manufacturer for product knowledge. [L1396.055]
6. Have them write collection page content to learn the products. [L1396.055]
7. Role-play difficult customer calls. [L1396.055]
8. Send mystery shoppers to chat or call them to practise answering. [L1396.055]
9. Monitor their chats and send private corrections during the first months. [L1396.055]

- Ask manufacturers for product training with a set meeting, because they are often willing to share their expertise. One manufacturer offered a one-hour Zoom training per product category once a week for five weeks, which the owner used to train staff directly. Schedule the session in advance so the expert is prepared. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1396.057]

### Skill transfer for a single task

#### Results

- Without a structured handover, the trained person often reaches only about 60-70% of the original efficiency. With the skill-transfer process, the target is to keep it at about 95-98%. [L1359.021]

#### Five-step process

1. **Explain:** Give a high-level walkthrough of the SOP, covering why the task matters, what the steps are and what the result should be. Do not demonstrate yet. [L1359.022]
2. **Demonstrate:** Perform the whole task yourself, narrating each step, so the trainee sees it done correctly the first time. [L1359.022]
3. **Practise:** Have the trainee go through the steps one by one while you sit beside them and correct each step right away. Depending on complexity, this takes 30 minutes to an hour, or several days. [L1359.022]
4. **Observe:** Let the trainee complete the entire process alone without interrupting. If they get stuck, finish the step with them before moving on to the next one. [L1359.022]
5. **Feedback:** Have the trainee explain what they do at each step and why. This shows whether they understood it and how comfortable they felt with the training. Use that feedback for the next handover. [L1359.022]

#### Common mistakes

- Skipping the explain step means the trainee does not understand why a step matters, so they follow it badly or skip it. Even simple tasks need the bigger picture. [L1359.023]
- If the trainer has never done the process, the demonstration step falls apart and the trainee learns the wrong way. Perform the process yourself first. [L1359.024]

#### Taking over a task (older)

*(Older: 2021.)* Use this procedure to train a new hire to take over a task:

1. Work alongside the new person through the day, covering the tasks that are too hard to put in an SOP. [L0469.107]
2. Once they are comfortable, have them do the task while you watch. [L0469.107]
3. Then have them do the task alone while you check in from time to time. [L0469.107]

- Plan on about a month of doing two jobs while training. *(Older: 2021.)* [L0469.107]

### How long training takes

Sources give different durations because they describe different roles and tasks. Use the row that matches your situation.

| Situation | Duration | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Single task, skill transfer (practice step) | 30 minutes to an hour, or several days, depending on complexity | [L1359.022] |
| New VA, initial training | Up to two weeks, ending earlier when practical checkpoints are passed | [L1473.022] |
| New VA, fixed two-week plan | Two weeks of daily routine with the owner before working alone | [L1396.055, L1396.056] |
| Source C hire, close involvement | One to two weeks | [L1431.023] |
| Taking over a task, doing two jobs | About one month | [L0469.107] |
| Customer service or fulfilment hire, questions | First few weeks, possibly up to a month | [L1277.119] |
| Source A VA, intensive work | First few months | [L1437.025] |
| Outsourced staff, full quality | About one to two months to reach the owner's quality *(older: 2021-22)* | [L1247.006] |

- Keep expectations low during the first week. [L0765.012]
- Do not expect to leave the business unattended right after hiring. [L1000.039]
- Monitor chats and send private corrections during the first months. *(Older: 2021-22.)* [L1396.055]

### Example case

- A candidate from Nigeria with little e-commerce experience but a good attitude and strong writing skills was hired at 200 USD per month. She received one month of training with video lectures, recorded sessions and onboarding. She then took on influencer outreach, research and several other tasks, and her pay was raised to 500 USD per month. [L1431.021]

### Customer service and fulfilment hires

*(Older: 2020.)* Use this procedure when a store outgrows the owner's time and a customer service or fulfilment hire is needed:

1. Document every customer service and fulfilment step for each supplier and each scenario before the hire starts. [L1277.119]
2. Set up an open communication channel with the new person, such as Slack or Facebook Messenger. [L1277.119]
3. Expect a stream of questions for the first few weeks, possibly up to a month, because the hire will not know every answer yet. [L1277.119]
4. Keep answering those questions until the process runs smoothly without you. [L1277.119]

### Scripts and customer reply guidelines

#### Scripting replies

1. Work intensively with the new VA for the first few months. [L1437.025]
2. Script every customer email you would write yourself, and have the VA copy the scripts into a spreadsheet. [L1437.025]
3. Once the VA drafts emails on their own, edit them and let them see your changes, so they learn where you adjust. [L1437.025]
4. Adapt wording to the local market. For example, use "mum" instead of "mom" for Australian customers, and check other region-specific terms. [L1437.025]

#### Reviewing replies

1. Set aside about 10 minutes per day in the first one to two weeks. [L1431.024]
2. Open the customer emails that the assistant has sent. [L1431.024]
3. Check how each reply is worded and whether it fits how you want customers to be addressed. [L1431.024]
4. Give feedback on anything that does not match, and confirm it is corrected. [L1431.024]

#### Writing for other countries

- When hiring for a different country, do not rely on machine translation for customer messages. Write a guideline for each typical situation in the customer's language and politeness level, because a literal translation can sound disrespectful. [L1431.025]

### Authority, access and control

#### Authority and policies

- Define all dos and don'ts for the business, including what authority the assistant has and which important policies must be followed. [L0765.012]

#### Access during training

- Give the new VA a login to the shared workspace and a video explaining each bookmark and dashboard. [L1473.023]
- Record screen-share videos for procedures without training material and store them on the shared drive. [L1473.023]

#### Ongoing control and reporting

- Set deadlines, check completed work, give feedback, and agree on the reporting rhythm: daily, weekly or monthly. [L0765.012]

## Managing the team: communication, performance and offboarding

### Management framework and the manager's role

- Managing staff is a separate skill set from building a store, so plan to learn it before scaling the team. *Older advice (2022).* [L0803.083]
- Outsourcing is not a magic fix. After hiring, your job changes to managing that person, so budget time for management rather than expecting them to run the business alone. [L1515.029]
- Outsourcing does not mean being absent. The owner must stay reachable for questions, and a short daily check-in is enough. Communication runs through messaging tools such as Skype and Slack, with a video or verbal catch-up roughly every two weeks. [L1122.024]
- Give criticism in a clear, factual and professional way with few emotions, the same way you would to an employee. Personal criticism can make spouses and partners feel attacked, which reduces cooperation. [L0832.013]
- Hire A players and remove C and D players from the team. Many owners who believe they have B players in fact have C and D performers, and this is the key to making the team work. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1334.020]

**Four management stages**

- Manage each new hire in four stages: onboarding, communication, performance review and rewards. Set clear expectations for each stage. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1054.004]
- The four stages only need to meet basic principles, so you do not have to follow one exact method. Meeting those basic needs at each stage is enough for successful outsourcing. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1054.005]

### Working hours and availability

- Set fixed schedules for all team members so they know when colleagues are available to collaborate. Example: 8 AM Eastern to 12 PM, 2 PM to 6 PM Pacific. Communicate clearly which hours each person works so the team can rely on each other and collaborate in Asana and Slack. [L0263.004]
- Flexible working hours are acceptable as long as the assigned tasks get done, for example starting early and leaving later around an appointment. Watch whether the task list is completed rather than how many hours are logged. [L0337.025]
- Set business hours for phone and messages. The VA answers calls Monday to Friday, 9:00 to 17:00. Messages arriving outside those hours are handled the next business day, which prevents the owner from working around the clock. [L1473.027]
- Time zones with Pakistan-based assistants: Switzerland is about 3 hours ahead of Pakistan, and the UK is about 4 to 8 hours ahead, so plan overlapping working hours accordingly. [L1432.031]
- How the hour-related sources fit together: fixed schedules (L0263.004) define when colleagues can collaborate, and flexibility (L0337.025) applies to the rest of the time as long as the tasks are done. The sources do not describe a conflict between them. [L0263.004, L0337.025]

### Reply windows and contact rules

**Why reply windows matter** *(older advice, 2021-22)*

- Letting staff call you whenever they want interrupts your focus in the same way that unmanaged customer calls do. Replacing one constant interruption with another gains nothing. [L1054.016]
- Answering questions the moment they arrive breaks your workflow and lowers productivity. Set the reply habit from day one, because a habit of constant availability is hard to reverse later. [L1054.018]
- Most questions from virtual assistants do not need an instant answer, and about 80% can wait for your scheduled reply times. [L1054.042]

**Setting the windows**

| Setting | Rule | When it applies | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| One to two fixed times per day | Set the times when you answer questions, and make clear that replies will not be immediate. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.015] | General rule for any team | Source B, 2021-22 |
| Start and end of the day, or middle and end | Agree on the windows during the end-of-onboarding call and keep them consistent from the first day. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.042] | Setting the windows during onboarding | Source B, 2021-22 |
| Phone Monday to Friday, 9:00 to 17:00 | Calls are answered in these hours. Messages outside them are handled the next business day. [L1473.027] | Business hours for phone and messages | Source A |
| Short daily check-in | A short daily check-in is enough for the owner to stay reachable. [L1122.024] | Daily contact | Source A |

**Emergency exception**

- Allow immediate contact only in real emergencies, such as a family emergency that stops a key staff member, like the main customer service contact, from working. For everything else, most questions from assistants can wait for your scheduled reply times. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.017]
- Only a real emergency, such as a key staff member being unable to work, justifies immediate contact. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.042]

**Channels and group chat**

- Communicate mainly through written messages using tools such as Skype or Slack, with WhatsApp as a backup. Make sure the whole team can reach each other. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.014]
- Day-to-day communication with a VA or freelancer should be about 80% written messages, not phone or video calls. Reserve calls for rare cases because they interrupt work and take more time. *Older (2021-22).* [L1055.031]
- Create one group chat for the whole team in Skype or Slack. Staff, especially in the Philippines, value feeling part of a team, so the group chat builds connection between people who work for you. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.019]

**Reply template** *(older, 2021-22)*

The following message is a template for telling the team when you will reply. Use it unchanged or adapt the time to your own reply window.

```
Good morning everyone, I hope you are all doing well and have a great start to the day. I will check messages again around 3 pm today, so please send anything urgent before then. Have a good day.
```

[L1054.020]

### Team culture and communication between members

- Encourage team members to communicate with each other, not only with the owner. This makes the group feel like a team instead of a set of people who each receive a few tasks per week. [L0413.014]
- Build a team culture where members propose unconventional ideas daily. Instead of saying no automatically, evaluate each idea based on its risk and potential downside. If the risk is low, test the idea and measure its impact on the brand. This leadership behavior keeps the team ahead of trends and enables genuine innovation. [L0260.028]
- When staff or contractors suggest ways to make a repeated task more efficient, take the suggestion seriously instead of shutting it down. Ignoring such input keeps inefficiencies in place and discourages people from improving the work. [L1039.036]
- Tell the team that mistakes are acceptable if they are reported honestly and owned. Respond to errors by simplifying the process so the same mistake is less likely, rather than by getting angry. [L1437.026]
- Including freelancers in team meetings gives them a sense of belonging and makes them feel part of a business worth being proud of. This loyalty helps the business in return. [L1000.072]

### Daily routine

**Daily VA routine**

1. The VA signs in each morning and posts a good-morning message stating availability and the day's plan. [L0921.017]
2. The owner reviews the plan and gives direction. [L0921.017]
3. Both sides keep a back-and-forth conversation during the day, and the owner asks promptly what the VA is working on if no reply comes. [L0921.017]
4. The owner asks about the VA's day to build the relationship and keep them engaged. [L0921.017]

**Daily communication and trust**

- Communicate with virtual assistants every day, for example through Slack. Once trust is established, give them authority to decide and fix problems without asking first, and review their decisions at the end of the month. [L1441.031]

### Meetings and team rhythm

| Meeting | Frequency and format | When it applies | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Team meeting | Twice a week, Monday and Thursday mornings. *Older (2019).* [L0356.061] | Small team of assistants | Source A, 2019 |
| Team calls | At least two per week to answer questions, give new tasks and discuss ideas. Experienced assistants can suggest ideas from their past clients. [L1431.026] | Team calls for questions, tasks and ideas | Source C |
| Weekly target meeting | Once a week, covering company and individual targets. *Older (2020).* [L1111.159] | Reviewing progress on goals | Source A, 2020 |
| Monthly team meeting | Once a month, for example on a Friday afternoon, once you have five or more people. Greet each other, congratulate the group, share upcoming opportunities and ask for feedback, not to criticize. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.026] | Team of five or more | Source B, 2021-22 |
| Monthly Kaizen meeting | Once a month, with the owner, all full-time assistants and all freelancers, including accounting and graphics contractors. [L1000.071] | Continuous improvement for the whole team | Source A |

The sources do not tie the meeting frequencies to one team size. Use the twice-weekly and two-calls-per-week rhythms for a working team of assistants, and add the monthly team meeting and monthly Kaizen once the team grows and includes freelancers. [L0356.061, L1431.026, L1054.026, L1000.071]

**Team meeting procedure** *(older, 2019)*

1. Hold a team meeting twice a week, on Monday and Thursday mornings. [L0356.061]
2. Appoint one assistant as team lead who attends every meeting. [L0356.061]
3. Check in with each assistant by call several times a week and review the current state of the business with them. [L0356.061]
4. Treat each assistant as a regular member of the company so they feel part of the team. [L0356.061]

**Monthly Kaizen procedure**

1. Hold the meeting once a month with the owner, all full-time assistants and all freelancers, including accounting and graphics contractors. [L1000.071]
2. Ask each person what is working, what is not working and what should be done to fix it. [L1000.071]
3. Turn the answers into actions for the business. [L1000.071]

### Reporting structures

- Require daily reporting from direct reports (your direct employees). Use Asana, a doc file, or written bullet points. Any system is fine, but check it consistently. If you don't check reports, people stop trying. Review, verify, and give feedback on progress so people stay accountable and improve. [L0263.022]
- Create different reporting structures by role. Each role needs metrics that drive behavior and visibility. [L0263.025]

| Role | Reporting | Metrics |
|---|---|---|
| Sales team | Daily performance reports, incentive-based compensation | Daily performance [L0263.025] |
| Product listing | Monthly or quarterly metrics | Products listed, product options improved [L0263.025] |
| Operations | Order processing metrics, task completion in Asana | Order processing, task completion [L0263.025] |

**Asana order reporting procedure** (Asana Reports, paid version, $10 per user)

1. Create a task for every order. Task title = order number + customer name/email. [L0263.026]
2. A single team member owns each order from placement through delivery. [L0263.026]
3. Others collaborate via task comments (shipping updates, supplier issues, etc.). [L0263.026]
4. The Reports tab automatically shows completed orders (last 4 weeks, 7 days, yesterday), open tasks per person, and can be filtered by individual. [L0263.026]
5. Show team members their completed tasks and open task count to keep them motivated and aware. [L0263.026]
6. Show the operations manager only the Upcoming tasks next 8 weeks view, hidden from the team, to see who is overloaded and needs help. [L0263.026]

### KPIs, OKRs and targets

- Each team member needs a clear mission, company goals and a personal KPI. Review progress in a weekly meeting covering both company and individual targets. *Older advice (2020).* [L1111.159]
- Set OKRs (Objectives and Key Results) for 6 to 12 month periods. Conduct check-ins and reshape them every 6 months based on progress. [L0260.019]
- OKRs align your team to the business vision and leadership goals while creating transparency across the organization about priorities. [L0260.020]
- Plan staff tasks three to six months ahead by setting aside a full working day for the planning session. Load each person's weekly targets through the end of the year so they know their work well in advance. Planning this far out reduces daily decisions about what staff should do next. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1396.072]

**When to set output targets**

| Work | Target rule | When it applies | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Product listing | Do not set output targets at first, because complexity varies by brand. With only one lister you cannot benchmark. With 2-3 listers on similar brands, compare their output to set realistic standards. Use your own time as a baseline: a specialist should do the job faster because listing is their only focus. [L0263.038] | New listing team, no benchmark yet | Source A |
| Content | Set clear weekly targets, such as twelve content items per week. Only follow up when weekly targets are missed. *Older (2021-22).* [L1396.052] | Content work with a clear weekly count | Source B, 2021-22 |
| Social media manager | Measure by engagement with posts and by collaboration with suppliers and niche partners, not by daily post count or follower target alone. *Older (2021).* [L0801.156] | Social media roles | Source A, 2021 |

### Measuring performance

**Measure results, not hours**

- Monitor virtual assistant performance by output (all customer emails answered, all tasks completed) rather than hours worked. [L0230.023]
- Measure the VA by results rather than hours: as long as all emails, messages and calls are handled, the exact working hours and breaks are not tracked. Downtime can be filled with assigned tasks in Asana. [L1473.028]
- Manage remote team members by results: check whether the task is done, not by screenshots or time tracking. If output is repeatedly late, first check whether the training or instructions were unclear before concluding the person is not right for the role. [L0337.023]
- Heavy time-tracking software such as Time Doctor did not work well with part-time staff, who often stopped using it or left. Daily reports and detailed tracking created friction without improving results. Measure weekly output instead of tracking hours. *Older (2021-22).* [L1396.051]
- Measure staff by weekly results instead of the process they use. If the agreed number of content items or uploads is finished by the end of the week, the method does not matter. Only follow up when weekly targets are missed. *Older (2021-22).* [L1396.052]

**Judging speed and effort**

- Before judging a remote helper's speed, estimate how long the task takes you to do well, including photos and details. If a task that takes you about one hour is still unfinished after half a day, the cause is usually unclear training or a misunderstanding, not necessarily laziness. Check the instructions and the training video first, then decide. [L0337.044]
- A staff member who reported ten or twelve hours for a task that produced only a few items in a week showed poor time estimates. Ask for an explanation of time spent and consider replacing staff whose output does not match their reported effort. Clear expectations at the start prevent this. *Older (2021-22).* [L1396.054]

**Detail orientation**

- Repeatedly missing details, such as missing tags or meta descriptions in product listings, is a sign of low detail orientation. After several repeated misses despite clear instructions, replace the team member. [L0337.024]

### Reviews and performance improvement plans

**Review cadence**

- Hold regular review catch-ups with each team member to review their work, raise improvement points, recognize good work, cover changes to systems or processes and let them suggest improvements. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.022]
- Review frequency: start with a review about every two weeks, then move to monthly, and once a person is long-term you can reduce it to quarterly. A half-hour meeting is usually enough. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.024]
- Set regular review dates with each team member about once per month, as the default cadence. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.023]

**Reviewing work procedure** *(older, 2021-22)*

1. Set regular review dates with each team member individually, about once per month, and put them in your calendar and theirs. [L1054.023]
2. If you have a larger team, also set a time for team meetings. These are more general and cover reviewing work and seeking feedback on processes and opportunities. [L1054.023]
3. Stay positive and focus on developing people, not punishing them. [L1054.023]
4. Where performance is below standard, ask why and what the individual needs to improve, then give them time to improve. [L1054.023]
5. If improvement is not visible, end the arrangement. [L1054.023]

**Individual review meeting procedure** *(older, 2021-22)*

- First catch up on the person and their life, such as their family, studies or other commitments. Then go through a prepared agenda of points to discuss, and if they are doing well, say so clearly and ask for any further improvement suggestions. [L1054.025]

**Performance improvement procedure** *(older, 2021-22)*

1. At the end-of-month performance meeting, identify one aspect of the work to improve. [L1054.029]
2. Agree on a plan together by asking what they need to make the improvement happen. [L1054.029]
3. Set a target, such as improving that area by the end of the next month, and confirm they understand and accept it. [L1054.029]
4. Give them the time to improve. For a high-impact task where poor performance cannot last, use a shorter period. [L1054.029]
5. If improvement is not visible, end the arrangement. [L1054.029]

**Improvement window**

| Situation | Window | Rule | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Default | One month | The person does not have to meet every target, but they must show substantial improvement against what was set. Whether they did enough is a judgment call. [L1054.030] | Source B, 2021-22 |
| High-impact task | Shorter than one month | Poor performance cannot last on this task. [L1054.029] | Source B, 2021-22 |

### Monitoring and tracking

**How the monitoring units fit together**

| Stage | Monitoring approach | When it applies | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| New hire, first days | Track calls, chats and response times from day one. Record calls where legal and monitor them closely at the start. *Older (2019) for the tracking point.* [L0467.068, L0910.056] | Start of employment | Source A, 2019 and Source A |
| Uncertain about a VA or new to working together | Use time monitoring. It is not recommended as a long-term measure because it can damage trust. [L0921.022] | Unsure about a VA or someone new | Source A |
| After the hiring process or once training is complete | Give full trust and avoid monitoring screens. Trust remains unless they give you a clear reason to withdraw it. [L1000.041, L0910.056] | Passed hiring or completed training | Source A |
| Warning signs appear | Use monitoring software and fix problems early. [L1441.028, L1441.029] | Excuses, slipping attendance, leaving early | Source A |

Judge monitoring by the stage. Output and results remain the main measure throughout. [L0230.023, L0337.023, L1473.028]

**Recording calls**

- Staff who answer calls should be monitored closely at the start by recording calls where legal, then trusted once training is complete. Customer complaints that reach the store owner are a signal to act if performance drops. [L0910.056]

**Hubstaff**

- Hubstaff tracks virtual assistant work with periodic screenshots, an activity percentage and hours worked per day. Use the data to see how long tasks take, check activity levels, and reprioritize workload. [L1441.029]

**Asana visibility**

- Asana reporting shows team members their completed tasks and open task count, and shows the operations manager the upcoming task view that stays hidden from the team. [L0263.026]

**Flexible hours**

- For flexible hours, watch whether the task list is completed rather than how many hours are logged. [L0337.025]

### Absences, sickness, vacations and outages

| Situation | What to do | Source |
|---|---|---|
| VA is sick | The owner covers the work and the VA simply reports the absence. [L1473.026] The owner covers responsibilities to maintain service continuity. [L0230.024] | Source A |
| Planned vacation | The VA gives notice about two weeks in advance. The owner covers those days too, so no replacement hire is needed. [L1473.026] The owner covers responsibilities to maintain service continuity. [L0230.024] | Source A |
| Not present as expected | End the engagement and hire a replacement, applying a firm zero-tolerance policy. *Older (2021).* [L0469.104] | Source A, 2021 |
| Pattern of absences | Freelance virtual assistants often work for several companies at once, which can cause absences or fatigue. Consider this possibility when assessing a pattern. *Older (2021).* [L0469.105] | Source A, 2021 |
| Power or internet outage | Require the team member to alert the team from their phone. Short outages of about 15 minutes to an hour can be tolerated when the alert is sent. *Older (2021).* [L0469.106] | Source A, 2021 |

Note on the sources: a reported absence (sick or planned) is covered by the owner under the current rules [L1473.026, L0230.024]. The zero-tolerance rule for an absence that is not present as expected comes from an older source (2021) [L0469.104]. Consider the pattern rule before acting [L0469.105].

**Written working hours**

- Give each virtual assistant written working hours and duties. If they are not present as expected, end the engagement and hire a replacement, applying a firm zero-tolerance policy. *Older (2021).* [L0469.104]

### Warnings, termination and hiring

**Termination for culture violations**

- Automatic termination for business culture violation: cheating (faking time tracking, falsifying reports), fraud, or dishonesty. No second chances. When trust is broken at this level, the person cannot stay. This is an automatic line in the sand; communicate it clearly during onboarding. [L0263.029]

**Yellow card procedure**

1. Note missed deadlines or quality problems, and refer to what was agreed. [L1431.029]
2. Send a written warning, called a yellow card, that states the expectation. [L1431.029]
3. If the problem repeats, end the work relationship. [L1431.029]
4. Only dismiss someone with stated reasons and prior warnings, and never for reasons such as lack of money. [L1431.029]

**Early termination and hiring**

- Expect to let go 30-35 people during the journey to building a 35-person team. Most early terminations are because of inadequate onboarding, unclear SOPs, or poor standards. As you improve systems, processes, and expectations, fewer people fail. The mistakes are yours; learn from them and improve the hiring and onboarding process. [L0263.030]
- If a virtual assistant does not work out despite careful hiring, let them go early rather than late, because a quick replacement costs less than a prolonged poor fit. Hire slowly and dismiss quickly. *Older (2019).* [L0356.062]
- Repeatedly missing details despite clear instructions leads to replacement. [L0337.024]

**When to act**

| Problem | Action | Timing | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cheating, fraud or dishonesty | Automatic termination, no second chances [L0263.029] | Immediately | Source A |
| Missed deadlines or quality problems | Yellow card, then end the relationship if it repeats [L1431.029] | After a stated written warning | Source C |
| Repeated detail misses despite clear instructions | Replace the team member [L0337.024] | After several repeated misses | Source A |
| Below standard with no visible improvement | Performance improvement plan, then end the arrangement [L1054.029, L1054.030] | Default one month, shorter for high-impact tasks | Source B, 2021-22 |
| Poor fit despite careful hiring | Let them go early [L0356.062] | As soon as the poor fit is clear. *Older (2019).* | Source A, 2019 |
