| Output late | Check whether the training or instructions were unclear first [L0337.023, L0337.044] | Before judging the person | Source A |

### Offboarding

**Ongoing staff offboarding procedure** *(older, 2021-22)*

1. Before telling them, remove their access to everything, ideally overnight, including Shopify, Google Drive and any other accounts. [L1054.031]
2. Remove access to shared payment cards, and order a new credit card if the person had card information. [L1054.031]
3. Remove access to the phone system if they had it. [L1054.031]
4. Remove them from all team group chats. [L1054.031]
5. Send a short written message saying the employment is ending because the required improvement did not happen, referring to the earlier discussion. [L1054.031]
6. Pay out any remaining pay. Do not hold a call to explain the decision. [L1054.031]

**Project-based freelancers**

- For project-based freelancers rather than ongoing roles, you can simply stop giving them work without a conversation or message. *Older (2021-22).* [L1054.032]

## Pay, bonuses, retention and contracts

### Pay levels by role

- **Full-time offshore VA in the Philippines (older: 2019):** typically 400 to 500 USD per month. Paying slightly above the common rate attracts candidates with corporate experience and reduces turnover. [L0356.057]
- **Full-time virtual staff via online-jobs.ph (older: 2020):** about 300 USD per month to start, rising to about 350, 400 or 500 USD per month for experienced staff. [L0852.103]
- **Full-time VA after proving themselves (no year given):** about 200 USD per week, based on the speaker's experience with Philippine VAs. The source gives this figure per week, so it is not directly comparable with the monthly figures above. [L0921.026]
- **Product lister, starting salary (no year given):** about 500 USD per month. This is lower than customer service because product listers do not interact with customers and have less variable demand. [L0263.037]
- **Routine VA work at about 10 USD per hour (no year given):** tends to produce lower quality and team members who leave. [L0413.011]
- **Where the sources differ:** the 2019 range (400 to 500 USD) describes a full-time offshore VA with corporate experience, while the 2020 range starts at 300 USD and rises with experience. Both apply to Philippine full-time hires, at different career stages and with different candidate profiles. [L0356.057, L0852.103]
- **Target level (no year given):** pay VAs close to US minimum wage levels, treat them well and build a team culture. [L1437.032]

### Pay above the market

- Pay virtual assistants above market rates to attract candidates with strong English and previous experience, because higher pay reduces the risk of poor performance. [L0230.013]
- Pay above the cheapest available rate to improve output quality and consistency and to keep people for years. [L0413.011]
- Pay a good VA somewhat above the typical Philippine rate to secure quality and loyalty; strong, engaged VAs are then willing to take on additional tasks for other businesses, even after the original store is sold. [L0921.019]
- Pay above the average rate for the role. A second hire at average pay produced disappointing candidates; raising the offer by about 100 USD per month brought much better applicants. [L1473.018]

### Pay models: per task, per product, per article and hourly

**General rules**

- Pay per task or per deliverable, such as per product description, per word or per batch of 500 images, instead of hourly. Hourly pay gives no incentive to work efficiently. Exception: ongoing work such as social media management. [L1122.019]
- Offer a bonus for finishing a per-task job within a set time. This motivates the freelancer to work efficiently. [L1122.020]
- Budget first, then check value: decide what you can afford, then pay more for tasks that deliver high value. [L1122.021]
- Set budgets by what you can afford rather than by a fixed rule, and judge price by quality tested through the trial process. Higher spending often yields better quality, but some good writers charge less. [L0379.017]

**Procedure: working out pay for a freelancer or VA (no year given)**

1. Set the budget first: decide what you can realistically afford. [L1122.021]
2. Assess the value the task delivers, such as whether it produces a big jump in profit, and pay more for high-value tasks. [L1122.021]
3. Research the market on platforms like Upwork to see what freelancers ask and what the average pay is, for example for a VA in the Philippines. [L1122.021]
4. Negotiate with freelancers, who are often willing to split work or adjust scope to fit your budget. [L1122.021]

**Written content and video (older: 2021 to 2022 for Source B; 2019 for Source A where marked)**

- Outsourced content work should always be paid per finished project, never per hour, because hourly pay gives no incentive to finish quickly and can encourage dishonest freelancers to slow down. Per-word pay for written content is an accepted alternative. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L0379.002]
- Treat each content piece as piece work: agree a price for the delivered finished piece and negotiate it before starting. Apply the same logic to video editing by agreeing a price per video based on its length and requirements. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L0379.003]
- Pay writers a fixed price per article rather than an hourly rate, because hourly pay makes costs hard to predict when some writers are slow. Quality matters more than volume. [L0382.011]
- Outsourcing a 500-word article to a non-native English writer on Odesk cost about 5 USD per 500 words at the time described. This is a rough rate; verify current freelancer rates before you budget. (older: rate from an earlier period, no year given) [L0380.020]

**Product uploads**

- Pay upload staff per completed product or per task instead of a fixed salary when output varies. Some part-time upload staff earned more than a full-time hire in busy months, and pay followed the work completed. This keeps costs tied to output. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1396.040]
- One VA was paid per product uploaded, not hourly, and earned roughly 800 to 900 USD per month during a busy period with many new suppliers. In slower months the same VA earned roughly 300 to 400 USD per month. [L0832.045]
- Calculate the rate per product before setting payment: rate per product = total payment divided by number of products. Multiply the number of products by the rate to get the payout. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1396.039]
- Worked example: a 200 USD test task of 179 products gives a rate of about 1.12 USD per product (200 ÷ 179), not 1.16 USD as the lesson stated. Recalculate the rate before setting payment. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1396.039]

**Hourly pay: where it fits**

- For reformatting a supplier CSV into Shopify's import format, a freelancer who already knows CSV files can be paid about 20 USD per hour. The work takes roughly 20 minutes to one hour however many products the file holds, so an hourly rate is cheaper than a fixed price per product. (older: 2019) [L1275.073]
- Price a VA task by checking whether the data is ready to upload. A CSV that only needs reformatting takes about 20 minutes, while scattered data entered one by one can take dozens of hours. Hire only people with many reviews for the exact workflow. (older: 2021) [L1112.073]

**Pay models compared**

| Work type | Pay model | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Articles and written content | Fixed price per article, per finished piece or per word; never hourly | [L0379.002, L0382.011] |
| Video editing | Price per video by length and requirements | [L0379.003] |
| Product uploads, output varies | Per product: total payment ÷ number of products | [L1396.039, L1396.040, L0832.045] |
| CSV reformatting, effort fixed regardless of product count | Hourly (about 20 USD/hour in the 2019 source) or price by data readiness (about 20 minutes of work in the 2021 source) | [L1275.073, L1112.073] |
| Ongoing work such as social media management | Time-based (the exception to per-task pay) | [L1122.019] |
| Phone answering, low call volume | Commission, or flat monthly fee after the first months | [L0245.053, L0910.053] |
| Sales handling quotes or multi-unit requests | 1% of total sale price | [L0832.049, L0832.059] |

### Commissions and profit bonuses

**Principles**

- Do not pay staff only a percentage of sales and expect them to drive results alone. Use clear KPIs combined with point-based incentives that can be redeemed for money, time off or products. (older: 2020) [L1111.160]
- Performance-based bonuses and compensation motivate higher performance, especially for sales teams. Set clear rules about acceptable behaviour, such as collaboration rather than toxic competition, to prevent conflict while keeping the drive for results. [L0263.032]
- Use a profit-share or incentive model when hiring VAs with a documented process; this lets a small team test products faster. [L1194.020]

**Commission bases in the sources**

- A common commission for a VA is 1% of the total sales they are responsible for. Once you hire the right assistant, a small extra bonus in the paycheck helps keep them motivated. [L0829.026]
- Reward a sales-focused VA with 1% of the sales they generate. Track the commission by sales channel so it is easy to see who generated each sale. [L1000.042]
- Pay 1% of the total sale price only on customer exchanges that need a quote or multi-unit requests, or that run longer than two back-and-forth emails. Simple questions about stock, colour or shipping are handled as ordinary customer service without commission. [L0832.049, L0832.059] (merged: duplicate rules)
- Use net profit instead of sales volume where discounting is a risk. A bonus based on sales volume made VAs discount heavily to hit the target, which hurt profit. [L1437.029]
- Monthly VA bonus = 1% of net profit, paid to each VA. Example: a store with 10,000 USD net profit in a month pays each VA 100 USD as a bonus. [L1437.030]
- For large B2B orders, the VA can receive a bonus of 1% of the order value. This is a bonus, not a commission on sales. [L1473.029]

**Phone answering**

- Commission for phone support aligns agent incentives with sales outcomes. This structure works well for low call-volume businesses (2 to 5 calls per day), where hourly wages would be inefficient. (older: 2020) [L0245.053]
- Early on, one VA was paid 5 USD for each day the assistant answered the phone. Later the fee was a flat 150 USD per month for answering calls, which is roughly 5 USD per weekday. (older: no year given) [L0910.051]
- Flat-fee cost per weekday = monthly fee ÷ number of weekdays in the month. The lesson's 150 USD per month is about 5 USD per calendar day; with about 22 weekdays per month the cost is about 6.80 USD per weekday (150 ÷ 22 = 6.82, which the source rounds). [L0910.052]
- A phone-answering fee is worth paying because each sale-related call can be worth 50 to 300 USD, while the assistant may already be at a computer for other work. The lesson recommends a flat monthly rate rather than hourly or per-call pay after the first months. [L0910.053]

**Sales roles**

- Assign a designated representative for B2B deals and scale the commission with deal size. Large corporate deals may take months to close, so the commission should reflect that effort. [L0832.051]
- Reward strong deals with an extra bonus in the paycheck, in addition to commission. Good pay and recognition make team members work to close better deals. [L0832.052]
- **Drawing account for uncertain sales:** an early mail-sales hire started with a 25 USD weekly drawing account plus a 5% commission on mail sales. Once the commission earned far more than the base, the company replaced the arrangement with a fixed salary. Use a drawing account with commission while sales are uncertain, and switch to salary once the commission level is clear. [L1369.125]

**Pay models by role (table, older: 2020 and 2022 source)**

| Role | Pay model | Detail from the operator's team |
|---|---|---|
| Product upload VA | Per product uploaded | About 800 to 900 per month in a busy period with many new suppliers, about 300 to 400 in slower months |
| Customer service VA | Paid for the service, no commission | Simple questions only |
| Quote and multi-unit sales VA | 1 percent of the total sale price | Applies to quotes and multi-unit requests |
| Real estate sales VA | Hourly | Depends on the number of hours worked |
| B2B representative | Commission scaled to deal size | Large corporate deals can take months to close |

[L0832.060]

### Raises and career growth

**Timing of the first raise**

- Plan the first salary raise after 6 to 8 months and communicate this during hiring, so the employee knows growth is planned. This gives motivation and prevents a feeling of being stuck at a flat rate. [L0263.031]
- Give an annual raise after the first year, plus occasional bonuses. [L1473.029, L0230.022]
- In the 30-day procedure, raise pay if the work is good after 30 days (see the hiring section). [L0852.104]
- The sources give different first-raise points (30 days, 6 to 8 months, after the first year). None says which to choose; they are presented here as alternatives. [L0852.104, L0263.031, L0230.022, L1473.029]

**After the first raise**

- Tie future increases to performance, such as completing new projects or taking on responsibility, rather than to time. [L0263.031]
- Show each team member where they can grow, such as eventually managing other VAs, and share company goals with them. The speaker set a goal of about 10 employees for those who grow with the company. (older: 2020) [L0832.048]
- Build long-term relationships with the same staff instead of repeatedly training new people for the same task. Do not start with salaries you cannot afford. [L1431.031]

**Procedure: salary progression (no year given)**

1. Set a starting salary that was negotiated with the hire. [L1431.027]
2. Agree on salary goals with long-term staff and review them every month or year. [L1431.027]
3. Raise pay when the stores grow and the team member takes on more responsibility. [L1431.027]
4. If a bad month occurs, a loyal team may accept a short-term salary cut. [L1431.027]
5. Only pay raises the business can afford, based on calculated monthly profit. [L1431.027]

**Example of progression (no year given)**

- A team member started at 100 USD per month two to three years earlier and set goals with the owner each year. Pay reached 800 USD, then 1,500 USD per month, as the stores grew. [L1431.028]

### Bonuses, rewards and recognition

**Bonus types**

- Pay a small bonus after a strong month, give extra paid days off, fund relevant training and increase responsibility. These cost little relative to the value the team creates. [L1122.026]
- Reward large milestones with bonuses, such as an extra payment after 1,000 products uploaded, to keep staff motivated. Milestone bonuses increased loyalty without extra management effort. Plan these rewards in advance. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1396.041]
- Paying bonuses is fine. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1054.035]
- Paid time off is also a good way to recognise people. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1054.035]

**Equipment and workstations**

- **Hardware programme (older: 2020 source, no year given):** identify tools that slow down team productivity, for example single screens for an operations team processing 100+ orders per month. Purchase hardware such as screens, laptops and chairs and distribute them to the team. Result: a significant morale boost and measurable productivity improvement. This is worth the investment for core teams. [L0263.033]
- **Workstation programme:**
  1. Offer 50% company reimbursement, max 1,000 USD total purchase per employee. [L0263.034]
  2. The employee pays 50%. [L0263.034]
  3. The employee applies through the manager and provides the purchase link and price. [L0263.034]
  4. The owner approves each purchase. [L0263.034]
  5. The employee buys a laptop, chair, mouse or headset. [L0263.034]
  6. The company reimburses 50% after purchase. [L0263.034]
  
  This builds loyalty and ensures the team has proper equipment while sharing the cost. [L0263.034]
- Reward examples include buying a staff member new equipment such as a laptop, or paying for higher-level training that builds skills they can use later in other jobs and that strengthens their resume. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1054.037]

**Recognition**

- Thank and praise a partner or team member when a task is completed, because recognition motivates them to take the next task. Pair praise with a clear request for the next step. [L0832.014]
- Recognise and reward team members by giving feedback and listening to concerns. [L1122.026]
- Regular recognition of good work encourages people to do their best and builds loyalty. Recognise people and tell them directly when they do a good job. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1054.039]
- Build a personal relationship with your team, for example by asking about their safety and well-being during a crisis. Staff who feel valued are more willing to go beyond their job and stay longer. [L1122.027]
- Remote team members were flown to meet the owner in person for two nights during a trip abroad. The owner said this moved the working relationship to a higher level and made the team feel more like a unit. This is a low-cost way to strengthen a remote VA team. [L1437.041]

**Rewarding good work (older: 2021 to 2022)**

1. Always pay people on time. [L1054.035]
2. Where work is provided above your normal standard, find ways to reward it. [L1054.035]
3. Paying bonuses is fine. [L1054.035]
4. If you work with Filipino staff, pay the 13th month. [L1054.035]
5. Paid time off is also a good way to recognise people. [L1054.035]

**Warning on large rewards (older: 2021 to 2022)**

- Overly large rewards set expectations you may not be able to keep. Use rewards sparingly so the person does not come to expect the same big reward every time. [L1054.038]

### Paying on time and cash flow

- Pay team members twice per month, at the beginning and middle of the month. This provides consistent cash flow every 2 weeks and is predictable. Use a single system for all payments, for example from spreadsheet to payment processor, to maintain a standard. (older: no year given) [L0263.021]
- Always pay on time. For per-project work, pay as soon as the project is submitted unless you agreed a different arrangement. For weekly pay, pay on the same day each week at as close to the same time as possible. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1054.033]
- Staff with families depend on predictable income, and late or irregular pay makes it unlikely they will stay long term. Your agreement to pay is part of the deal, so you must meet it. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1054.034]
- Do not pay a freelancer in full before any work is delivered, especially on a platform without buyer protection, because you can be left with an unfinished task and no recourse. Choose platforms that hold payment until you accept the finished work, or pay in stages tied to deliverables. (older: no year given) [L1418.011]
- Structure hiring so that payment depends on delivered work. Paying freelancers upfront, or assigning only simple tasks before payment, can lose money when work is never delivered. (older: no year given) [L1433.002]

### Statutory obligations

- In the Philippines, the 13th month is a local requirement, similar to year-end holiday pay. Pay it to staff who have worked for you there for any period and are still with you at year-end. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1054.036]
- If you work with Filipino staff, pay the 13th month. (older: 2021 to 2022) [L1054.035]

### Hiring terms and trial periods

- Hire VAs on full-time contracts with exclusivity instead of 3 to 4 hours a day, because part-time staff tend to look for other full-time work and become distracted. State at hiring that they work only for your business. (older: 2019) [L0356.058]
- **Hiring and retaining virtual staff procedure (older: 2020):**
  1. Hire the first person on a 30-day contract. [L0852.104]
  2. Monitor their work and give proactive feedback to help them improve. [L0852.104]
  3. If the work is good after 30 days, raise their pay, for example from 300 to 350 USD per month. [L0852.104]
  4. Set shared goals, such as a bonus when monthly sales pass a set amount. [L0852.104]
- Virtual staff for full-time roles can be found on online-jobs.ph for about 300 USD per month, with pay rising to about 350, 400 or 500 USD per month for experienced staff. (older: 2020) [L0852.103]
- OnlineJobs.ph is used to hire VAs, including staff in the Philippines. Many hires in the team stayed for five or more years. (older: 2022) [L0803.081]
- Prefer a long-term assistant who knows the store and processes. The speaker's team had worked together for four years; once trained, the team can run ad campaigns, so the founder only reviews campaigns and makes decisions. [L1432.033]
- Starting with an assistant you pay out of revenue can work when cash is short. Move to a monthly fixed salary once revenue exists. [L1432.030]
- Calling outsourced helpers team members or employees instead of virtual assistants supports a culture where people stay long term. The framing matters most when the owner is hiring for ongoing roles rather than short tasks. (older: 2022) [L1281.129]

### Contracts

- Execute a formal written contract before the virtual assistant begins work. [L0230.017]
- Before a VA starts training, sign a written contract. A generic VA contract can be found online and adapted to the situation. The contract should state pay, weekly work hours, holidays observed, vacation notice, notice period for quitting, and the training obligations. [L1473.038]
- Use simple contracts for team members in the Philippines, Bangladesh and South America. Standard templates (LegalZoom or similar) are sufficient. Contracts are not always necessary from day one; some team members ask for them later for credit or visa purposes. This is not standard practice but helpful when requested. (older: no year given) [L0263.046]
- Virtual assistant contracts: a formal contract is easier to set up when a hiring platform is used. A private arrangement should be based on trust and a clear revenue split. [L1432.030]
- Test products with a VA using a documented process and an incentive model, so a VA can check products against the criteria list and free the operator's time for decisions. [L1194.020]

### Employment classification

- Hiring ongoing staff in the country where the business is registered can lead the government to treat them as employees rather than contractors. This creates obligations that small businesses often cannot manage, which is a reason to start with overseas workers. This applies in the US, Australia and the UK. [L1122.017]
- Use contract forms and terms that match the worker's status (see Contracts above; simple contracts for Philippine, Bangladeshi and South American team members). [L0263.046, L1122.017]

### Keeping staff long-term

- Retain good team members for as long as possible. Turnover wastes the time invested in training and in knowing the suppliers, market and customers. Keep expectations stable and change them only with a conversation. [L1122.025]
- Build long-term relationships with the same staff. Do not start with salaries you cannot afford. [L1431.031]
- Staff who feel valued are more willing to go beyond their job and stay longer. [L1122.027]
- Pay VAs close to US minimum wage levels, treat them well and build a team culture. Good pay and treatment keep turnover low and produce much more committed work. [L1437.032]
- Paying a good VA somewhat above the typical Philippine rate secures quality and loyalty. [L0921.019]
- Predictable pay and timing make staff more likely to stay long term. [L1054.034]
- A loyal team may accept a short-term salary cut in a bad month. [L1431.027]
- Show growth paths and share company goals so staff can see a future with the business. [L0832.048]

## Team structure, roles and work tools

### Ownership and accountability

- The RACI matrix assigns four role types to every task and function:

| Role | Meaning |
|------|----------|
| Responsible | The person who does the work |
| Accountable | The person accountable for the outcome |
| Consulted | People whose input and ideas are sought |
| Informed | People kept in the loop |

  Map every deliverable across your team to these roles. Simplify and review whenever you add a channel, make a new hire, or change a process. [L0260.011]
- A RACI matrix prevents confusion and competing priorities by creating clear ownership and structured handoff sequences. Without it, multiple team members pursue conflicting directions for the same task. [L0260.012]
- Each new hire takes over the backup role and tasks of the previous hire. This frees the owner and gives the team a clear chain of responsibility. Continue handing out tasks until the owner is only doing sales and high-level work. [L0337.019]
- Shared tools make the business transparent. The owner can see order status, check whether emails are answered, read reports in Asana and the help desk, and reach the VA directly on Slack when something is open. [L1473.031]

### Team structure by revenue phase

- Phase guidance, with both thresholds from the sources and when each applies:

| Monthly sales | What applies | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| Up to roughly $500,000 | A virtual team is fine for drop shipping. An office and an in-house team are not needed. | L0413.009 |
| $20,000 to $100,000 | The business moves toward a real brand and marketing gains weight. A project manager takes over leading the team toward performance goals. | L1129.008, L1129.009 |
| Roughly $500,000 and above | The source gives the $500,000 point as the threshold for an office and an in-house team. It gives no separate rule above that level. | L0413.009 |

- Both thresholds apply together. A virtual team with a project manager is the expected setup at $20,000 to $100,000 per month, and the office threshold of about $500,000 comes later. [L0413.009, L1129.008, L1129.009]
- At $20,000 to $100,000 per month, a project manager monitors the customer service and influencer outreach staff against performance goals and can help hire new people. Expect to pay about $8 to $12 per hour on Monazar or Upwork. [L1129.009]
- Virtual teams stay connected by adding everyone to the same Slack channel, including virtual assistants doing different tasks such as product research and product image sourcing. This shows them that they are part of one company. [L0413.010]
- Define which team roles the business will need, such as ads managers, customer service reps, graphic designers and videographers. Decide whether these staff are based overseas or local in an office. *(Older advice: 2021-22)* [L0439.022]
- An operations manager can run the daily business, so the owner only checks in weekly. In a larger setup, one operations manager can oversee a network of businesses while the owner sits at the top. *(Older advice: 2021-22)* [L0439.023]
- A typical team structure, *(older advice: 2022)*:
  - The owner handles research and supplier connections plus ad management, 1 to 5 hours per week.
  - A business or operations manager oversees support and sales leads (sales lead and support lead).
  - Design and copywriting teams report directly to the business manager. [L0665.104]
- Keep one team across all stores rather than separate teams per store. Operations and marketing teams can serve every store. *(Older advice: 2022)* [L0665.105]
- A team of 10 virtual assistants and freelancers can cover supplier sourcing for high-ticket dropshipping, website design, product uploads, product optimization, product descriptions and graphic design, plus occasional smaller tasks. *(Older advice: 2021-22)* [L0499.004]
- A content-led brand can run with a small team. The owner films the videos, a video editor handles editing, a part-time social media person posts for about three hours per day, and a full-time designer handles visuals. Divide the work so the owner focuses on creating content. [L0487.064]
- When scaling a dropshipping business, split responsibilities across distinct roles:
  - Product research and scaling strategy
  - Branding and creative testing
  - Paid ads and funnel optimization
  - Operations, fulfillment and backend systems

  Each role covers a different part of the business, so the whole operation gets full coverage. [L0873.001]
- Operations-focused people who handle fulfillment and backend systems are also well placed to negotiate with suppliers and designers and to keep stores streamlined for sustainable growth. Assigning supplier negotiation to the person who runs operations keeps the fulfillment side and the supplier side aligned. [L0873.002]
- One company grew to about 15 people, split between the US and the Philippines. Roles covered web design, operations, social media and support, which was divided into live chat, phone and email. *(Older advice: 2020)* [L0852.102]
- As the business grows, managers handle daily tasks. The owner checks the business at a high level by tracking the main drivers of revenue, ad spend, return on ad spend, support response times and reviews. *(Older advice: 2021)* [L0962.131]

### Accountability chart and org structure

*(Older advice: 2021-22, Source B)*

- An accountability chart is an org chart that shows each role's responsibilities and who holds each person accountable. Draw it bigger than the current team so it shows the structure the business is growing into, and treat it as a living document. [L1334.017]
- In a Traction-style accountability chart, the top roles are called:
  - **Visionary**, who owns ideas, R&D, culture and market fit.
  - **Integrator**, who owns systems, the business plan and targets.

  These labels replace CEO and COO titles. [L1334.018]
- Every business has three components: sales and marketing, operations, and finance and admin. Each has a director, and the top leader should have only these three direct reports. [L1334.019]
- Split a combined role into two hires when the workload becomes too big for one person:
  - Customer communication goes to customer service.
  - Supplier work, order management and order processing go to order processing.

  A later split can separate sales from customer service when the business gets busy. [L1334.022]
- Accountability chart example. Costs are examples only. [L1334.027]

| Role | Reports to | Example cost per month | Accountable for |
|---|---|---|---|
| Visionary | Top | $20,000 | R&D, initial vendor relations, new products, market fit, culture, creative ideas |
| Integrator | Top | $10,000 | Systems and processes, business plan, EOS, setting and achieving targets |
| Sales and Marketing Director | Top | $3,000 | Sales and marketing strategy, sales training, revenue target |
| Marketing Manager | Sales and Marketing Director | $1,500 | Managing the marketing team |
| SEO specialist | Marketing Manager | $800 | On-page SEO, off-page SEO |
| PPC specialist | Marketing Manager | not specified | Managing paid ads |
| Content writer | Marketing Manager | not specified | Blog posts, emails and other copy |
| Sales rep (VA 1) | Sales and Marketing Director | $1,000 | Sales by phone, chat and email |
| Operations Director | Top | $3,000 | Customer experience, processes and systems |
| Customer Service Manager | Operations Director | $1,500 | Post-sale fulfillment, customer service, problem solving |
| Customer service VA | Customer Service Manager | $1,000 | After-purchase service, customer problems |
| Catalog Manager (VA 3) | Operations Director | $1,200 | Product catalog management, product listings |
| Order Processing VA (VA 2) | Operations Director | $600 | Order management, supplier coordination |
| Product Uploader | Operations Director | $500 | Data entry |
| Processes and systems | Operations Director | not specified | Creating, updating and streamlining processes, systems and training materials; scorecard reporting |
| IT and website | Freelancer as needed | not specified | Setup and maintenance of computers, website and software |
| Finance and Admin Director | Top | not specified | Finance management, bookkeeping, Profit First, CFO work, financial reporting, analysis, strategic planning, tax accounting and tax preparation |

- Once several marketing specialists such as SEO, PPC and content writers are in place, hire a marketing manager to coordinate them. This takes the owner out of implementing training material and day-to-day marketing work. [L1334.050]
- Add the expected team size to the business description for each horizon, because it is a concrete marker of growth. A 1-year picture might show a few employees, while a 3-year picture might show around 10 staff. Keep the description to a short overview. [L1519.019]

### Dividing roles and delegating

- Assign each team member the roles that match their strengths. One VA handled support and operations, while another with graphics and Shopify back-end skills built additional stores. *(Older advice: source year not stated)* [L1437.028]
- Split the workload across assistants rather than giving one person everything. For example, two assistants can each manage the same two stores, so no individual is overloaded. [L1431.030]
- Virtual assistants for live chat, phone calls and order shipping should be separated from social media and content roles, because one person handling all of them quickly breaks down. [L0970.153]
- Do not expect one virtual assistant to handle every task well. Group related tasks, for example email plus live chat plus phone, and define clear goals and tracking for each role. *(Older advice: 2022)* [L0803.082]
- Split influencer work across dedicated roles: one person for micro-influencers, one for mid-level and high-level deals, and one for content curation. Three people covered the influencer function at one brand. [L0790.081]
- A B2B sales VA with real estate experience was close to closing an order worth about 18,000 for a government buyer. The owner had planned to offer an extra 200 discount, but the VA reduced the discount and still closed the deal. Give experienced sales staff authority to set final pricing within agreed limits. [L0832.062]

### Hiring with managers

- Involve team managers and leaders in the hiring process from the beginning:
  1. Have them help create screening questions, read candidate answers, and push people through the hiring funnel in Asana.
  2. Have them conduct the first 10 to 15 minute interview call (short call).
  3. Keep the final decision for yourself.
- This gives leaders ownership of their team, involvement in culture, and investment in new hires. It is more effective than hiring a manager for an existing team. [L0263.024]
- A manager who has learned a documented hiring process can run a hire on their own, with the owner supplying the content. Expect to coach the manager closely on the first hire, and expect the second hire to be run without the owner. *(Older advice: 2021-22)* [L1334.049]

### Capacity measurement

- Measure operating capacity by asking the team how busy they are and whether orders, customer questions or supplier orders are slipping through. Late orders and forgotten supplier orders are signs that the current capacity limit has been reached. *(Older advice: 2021-22)* [L1334.045]
- Realistic capacity can turn out well above the first estimate once a strong team is in place. Reassess capacity as the team grows instead of relying on the initial number. *(Older advice: 2021-22)* [L1334.047]
- Measuring what a virtual assistant actually does reveals how much capacity they have. It is common to underestimate it and overload them. Track their workload with time data before assigning more tasks, and review the results when hiring additional assistants. [L1441.030]
- Time tracking tools such as Hubstaff and the Upwork time tracker log and monitor the working hours of remote team members. [L0926.009]

### Workflow boards and task management

- Create separate boards for workflows with different timelines, such as regular quotes, white glove installation quotes, product upload and content. White glove installation requests can come in at three or four per day and need separate follow-up with both the manufacturer and the customer. Keep a separate board for each workflow to avoid mixing them. *(Older advice: 2021-22)* [L1396.058]
- Monday.com (alternatives: Asana or Trello) is used for task management. It stores social media planners, works-in-progress, OKRs and daily deliverables in one accessible location. [L0260.015]
- Source A uses Asana for the hiring funnel and for team reports. The weakest part of the system is the manual updating of statuses, notes and due dates, because it depends on the VA remembering each update. Where possible, choose tools that update statuses, notes and due dates automatically. [L0263.024, L1473.034]
- Trello is a kanban-style project board used to track tasks, assign owners and manage recurring processes when a team or virtual assistants are involved. [L0817.005]
- Basecamp is a project management tool for team tasks. Its small plans are affordable for a startup team. It can track monthly promotions with assigned roles and responsibilities. *(Older advice: 2021)* [L0853.093, L0247.121]
- Google Drive stores day-to-day files and shared documents. *(Older advice: 2021)* [L0853.093]
- Grammarly is a free grammar and formatting tool for Gmail and other writing platforms. It catches repetitions, run-on sentences and spelling errors in professional email communication with suppliers. *(Older advice: 2021)* [L0270.020]
- Working roadmap prompt:

```
Turn the 30-day roadmap into a shorter working version. Give one line per action, grouped by week one, week two, week three and week four. Separate fixes to do now from testing ideas, and keep only the metric each action should improve. Then assign every action to a named team member with their responsibility.
```

  [L0886.036]

### Communication

- Slack is used for informal team communication and rapid information sharing. [L0260.016]
- Slack keeps distributed team members connected. Add everyone to the same channel, including virtual assistants doing different tasks, so they see that they are part of one company. [L0413.010]

### ClickUp for personal and team task management

- Set up ClickUp (free plan) for personal task management:
  1. Create separate Spaces for each area, such as life management, the e-commerce brand, and mentoring, with the brands kept in separate folders.
  2. In each list, add tasks with an assignee, a priority, a due date, and a description.
  3. Break tasks into subtasks and sub-steps.
  4. Use the List view as the default for simple to-do/done lists.
  5. Use the Board view for status-based pipelines such as creatives (Ideas, Scripting, Editing, Done).
  6. Use the Calendar view to see all tasks with due dates across the workspace.
  7. Filter the Everything view by priority to see the daily top tasks.
  8. Save a priority view and work from it when time is free.

  [L1181.018]
- ClickUp is a free task management tool with list, board, calendar and whiteboard views. It also has a Docs feature for dynamic documents that can turn text into tasks, custom fields, threaded comments, and a Gmail extension for turning emails into tasks. [L1181.019]
- ClickUp custom fields: add a custom field of type Drop Down with options such as strategy call, PDF, case study and video library so each deliverable shows its type. Add fields via the list's column settings or from the field library. [L1181.020]
- ClickUp views for different processes:

| View | Use for |
|---|---|
| List | Simple to-do lists |
| Board | Statuses (to do, done, or pipeline stages) |
| Calendar | Deadlines across lists |
| Whiteboard | Brainstorming creative hooks and angles |
| Docs | Processes and reference material |

  [L1181.021]
- When a team starts producing creatives or deliverables, use a status pipeline to track each item from idea to shortlist, scripting, storyboarding, editing and final. Move items across stages as they progress. [L1181.022]
- ClickUp Docs can turn a text item into a task. Select the text, create a task from it, add it to a board such as brand creatives, then set priority and due date. [L1181.023]
- Organize work by separating areas (brand, mentoring, life management). When a calendar block is set for one area, only tasks of that area appear, which avoids distraction from other work. [L1181.024]

## Delegating operations: customer service, fulfillment and uploads

### Principles for delegating operations

- Operational problems are unavoidable in dropshipping and e-commerce. Competitive advantage comes from solving them faster than competitors, which makes support responsiveness as strategically important as platform features. [L0216.011]
- Outsourcing is acceptable when the owner understands the process and can spot-check the work. Check that each product row matches its title, description and price before it goes live. [L1111.161] (Source A, 2020, older)
- Speed of response determines conversion in custom-product and B2B quote scenarios. Customers contact several competitors at once, and whoever responds first builds the relationship and wins the deal. [L0284.001]
- Outsourcing repetitive uploads to a freelancer is a reasonable way to save time, because uploading takes a long time, especially when suppliers send data in different formats. [L0966.089]
- Outsourcing does not remove upload time. Upload time grows with the number of products, so start early and have the catalogue ready before launch. [L1166.031] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- Do not treat one lesson as a complete outsourcing plan. Outsource a task such as product uploads only if it takes too much of your time, and first upload existing approved brands to go live before adding new suppliers. [L1115.076]
- Outsourced work that needs no deep local or cultural knowledge can go offshore. Work that depends on knowing the culture should stay with someone from that market. Example: a campaign that phoned households in rural Australia was staffed by offshore virtual assistants (VAs) working from a very basic script. [L1359.044]
- Outsourced product review or approval work may give unsatisfactory results. Verify quality before relying on an outsourced process. [L1094.109] (2023)
- Outsourcing customer service is among the first delegations to consider, because customer inquiries are a classic time drain for new owners and the workload grows with the business. [L1514.015] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- Concern that outsourced customer service will be worse is unfounded if the right people are hired. Outsourcing also protects the owner's stress level and brainpower. [L1247.007] (Source B, 2021-22, older)

### Staffing order and roles

**When to hand over customer service.** Sources differ on the trigger. Use both tests:

| Stage | Who handles support | Source |
|---|---|---|
| First months | Owner handles customer service for at least the first few months, because outsourcing is hard to do well without having done the work yourself (Source B, 2021-22, older) | [L0909.002] |
| Early phase, situations still unknown | Owner handles messages personally to learn which situations occur (Source C) | [L0911.003] |
| Live chat, early phase | Owner answers live chats personally to learn what customers ask and to respond quickly | [L0948.022] |
| Sales generate enough cash flow to pay for support | Outsource customer service; until then, keep answering calls, emails and chats yourself or through the callback system (Source B, 2021-22, older) | [L0909.017] |
| Owner is exhausted and sales are stuck | Owner handled support personally, which was mentally taxing and caused burnout. Hire a VA for email support as the first priority before scaling further (Source C) | [L1127.036] |
| Store is profitable and can afford a hire | Outsource live chat to a staff member | [L0948.022] |
| Main product is private-labelled | Owner should not handle customer service personally. Hire a team member to answer support contacts and log every customer request, complaint and feature idea (Source B, 2021-22, older) | [L1155.018] |

- Use cash flow as the test for whether you can afford the hire, and burnout or stuck sales as the signal that support is urgent. [L0909.017, L1127.036]
- A full-time customer service agent in the Philippines can cost as little as $400 per month (Source B, 2021-22, older). Even with an agent, you may still need to speak to some customers yourself. [L0909.018]

**First hire: one person for support and orders.**
- Hire one person for both customer service and order management. The tasks are closely linked; one person reduces time lag, can cancel orders directly, and has access to both suppliers and customers. [L1473.016]
- A single VA can often cover email, live chat and order processing for a young store, so one hire is usually the starting point. Phone support tasks can be handled by the same person, though roles are often split as the business grows. [L1430.001]
- Once order volume grows, hire a VA for phone calls, emails and daily operations. Train the assistant on the supplier notes spreadsheet before handing over the work. [L0468.171] (Source A, 2020, older)
- Hire an operational VA, for example for support tickets, phone calls and order processing, once business profits can pay for them. Test a new VA with a small project of 50 to 100 products before a large batch. [L1112.072] (2021, older)
- Assign duties in stages and check performance with time-tracking data. The assistant should work in the same hours as the team to reduce handover delays. [L1441.043]
- Route multi-unit requests, such as a customer asking for a dozen units, directly to the VA who handles sales. These deals take a long time and need one person to own them. [L0832.050]
- A VA can cover order fulfilment, phone contact with customers and product uploads, but only after meeting English and speed requirements. [L1441.043]

**Equipping the VA before the first day.** [L1430.003]
1. Ability to submit orders to suppliers.
2. Access to product information, so they can answer live chat and email questions.
3. Access to stock levels through weekly inventory reports and dealer logins, to check availability for potential customers.
4. Limited access to the Shopify admin panel, to look up order status.
5. A document with all supplier contact details, so they know whom to contact when an order arrives.
6. A standard order submission email template for suppliers.
7. Ability to enter tracking numbers in the Shopify back end when a supplier confirms shipment.

**Owner's role and roles for the team.**
- Owners should hear about a customer problem after it has been resolved, as long as it was resolved quickly. A customer service manager should handle post-sale problems so the owner does not answer the team's questions. [L1334.021] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- A catalog manager keeps product pages, listings and stock information current, adding new products and removing discontinued ones. Outdated information causes customer questions about wrong products and out-of-stock items, which pulls the service team off track. [L1334.023] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- Assign the thank-you task to the team member most relevant to it, usually the person who processes orders or a phone customer service team member. The two roles can be held by the same person or by different people. [L0406.027] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- Marketing and SEO are roles to hire for later, after profit thresholds are met. [L1446.033]

### Customer service and phone answering

**Phone staff requirements.**
- Staff who answer calls for an English-speaking store should be native English speakers when customers are in the US. Accents from some outsourcing countries can make US customers lose trust. [L0910.014]
- A phone VA must be a native English speaker and work during US business hours, for example 8 a.m. to 5 p.m. Assistants from other locations suit back-office tasks but not live calls with US customers. [L0910.045]
- The main requirement for phone staff is trust and friendliness. A warm, caring voice keeps customers calm even when the agent cannot answer every question. Agents still need written answer templates for common questions, such as shipping times. [L0910.015]
- Plan the first major phone-coverage milestone as 8 AM Eastern through late Pacific time (roughly 12 hours). This requires at least 2 people to share the shift. As volume grows, add a second shift with 2 people in the morning and 2 in the evening, which also gives backup when someone takes a day off. [L0263.009]
- A business number can later be set up so several staff members or VAs answer the same line. A single-owner setup is enough at launch. [L1198.011] (Source B, 2021-22, older)

**Commission-based phone VA (older model).** [L0245.052] (Source A, 2020, older)
1. On Craigslist or Fiverr, find a virtual assistant willing to answer phone calls on a commission basis.
2. Explain that your business phone number rings through to their personal phone, so they are doing other activities at the same time.
3. They answer incoming calls and attempt to make sales.
4. If they cannot answer a question, they take a message.
5. Pay commission on sales originating from phone calls (approximately 1 to 2% of sale value), not an hourly wage.

**Prevention before handling complaints.**
- A better strategy than handling more complaints is to prevent the failure that causes them. A small loss rate that is handled well can still create angry customers, so focus on the root cause. [L0910.043]

**Remote VA tasks.**
- Tasks that can be done remotely include invoicing, accounting updates, sending tracking notifications and fulfilling orders. A Philippines-based assistant can update accounting once a week, especially at month end. [L0910.044]

**Email, social and chat.**
- Hand email, social media messages and store chat to a VA only after you know exactly how to handle each situation and your budget allows it. [L0911.003]
- Set up a separate customer support email address that the assistant can access directly, so the founder is not replying to routine order questions. [L1432.006]
- Live chat should be installed on the store. Shopify Inbox is now a native Shopify option that replaces a separate live chat tool such as Tidio. Its companion phone app sends a notification whenever a customer writes, so a team member can reply quickly. [L1432.007]
- Give the customer support assistant login access to the live chat tool, either Tidio or Shopify Inbox, so they can answer visitors in real time. Fast chat replies are part of the support setup. [L1358.030]

**Why outsource fulfilment and support.**
- Once a store generates sales, outsource order fulfilment and customer support. Answering repeated "where is my order" emails consumes time better spent on new offers, brand building and creatives. [L1432.003]

### Order processing and fulfilment

**Order placement.**
- Keep order placement manual until stock is confirmed reliably. Order processing should move to a VA once volume grows. [L0834.105] (Source A, 2021, older)
- Submit first orders to the supplier as an email purchase order (PO) including supplier name, SKU, quantity and customer shipping address. For first orders, confirm the format with the supplier: "This is my first order; please let me know if you need anything else." [L0249.084] (Source A, 2022, older)
- Fulfilment work requires no special skill, so hire someone to place orders with the supplier. At high order volume, this person may also negotiate lower unit prices. [L1432.004]

**Order management workflow.** [L0230.007]
1. New order received → added to the Asana board → notification in Reamaze → fraud check by the Clearsale service → forwarded to supplier.
2. Order confirmed → credit card charged → updated in Asana.
3. Order shipped → tracking number added → status updated in Asana → marked incomplete until delivery.
4. Backorder → team communicates with customer and supplier → order moved to the backorder column in Asana.
5. Damages or returns → team communicates with customer and supplier → status updated in Asana.

**Access for fulfilment.**
- For order fulfilment, the assistant needs access to the payment providers and the supplier account, which should be set up at the start. Once the process is stable, the owner can stop checking every order and check periodically instead. [L1358.028]

**Daily fulfilment check.** [L1358.029]
1. At the end of each day, check whether the team has fulfilled all orders.
2. In the first weeks, check this closely.
3. When the routine runs as planned, reduce the check to a regular review.

**Backorders and stock-outs (case).** [L0359.104]
- A store owner had 12 sales in the first week, but the main supplier could not fulfil the orders.
- The response: update the product pages with a backorder notice, send confirmation emails, stop ads for backordered products, restructure the store to show in-stock best-margin products, and keep searching for more supplier approvals.
- The lesson: the problem was temporary, and the niche did not need to be abandoned.

**Supplier price and stock changes.**
- Review supplier price and product changes as they come in. Set stock to zero in the Shopify admin for products the supplier discontinued, and list new products that suppliers add. [L0962.132] (Source A, 2021, older)
- Keep stock and pricing under your own control. Update prices only when a supplier changes its MAP policy or wholesale cost, not daily. [L1090.137] (Source A, 2021, older)

**Sourcing a winning product through a VA.** [L0678.047] (Source C)
1. Find a winning product on a competitor's store.
2. Send the assistant the competitor's product link.
3. Ask the assistant to contact the supplier, for example CJ Dropshipping, and place the order for you.
4. Allow a few days for the supplier to reply, since supplier agents are slow to respond.

### Daily routines and board management

**Morning routine.** [L1396.015] (Source B, 2021-22, older; takes about one to two hours at a busy store)
1. Open email, chat, phone, Shopify and Trello.
2. Review the inbox first for urgent messages, such as customers who want to cancel an order.
3. Send the new orders to the manufacturers.
4. Contact abandoned checkouts.
5. Check all emails for tracking numbers, follow-ups and customer questions, and create quote requests where needed.
6. Finish with the Order Processing board: check backorders that are ready to ship, request tracking numbers, and check manufacturer confirmations.

**Board management.** [L0292.016] (Source A)
1. Review the board to identify cards delayed in any list longer than expected.
2. Monitor carrier tracking and move confirmed delivered orders to delivered and pending review.
3. Follow up with suppliers on orders in sent to supplier that have not shipped.
4. Contact customers in delivered and pending review to request reviews.
5. Move completed reviews to the reviewed column.

**Follow-up on quotes and delays.**
- When a quote depends on the manufacturer, follow up with the manufacturer as well as the customer. If the response does not arrive within two or three days, keep checking. Track both follow-ups on the same card so delays are not forgotten on either side. [L1396.059] (Source B, 2021-22, older)

**Inbox discipline.**
- Aim for inbox zero by the end of each day or as soon as possible, so that the assistant and owner know all messages have been handled. Clearing a full inbox daily is the goal of the labelling system. [L1000.069]
- A large unmanaged inbox, with thousands of emails read once and then forgotten, causes missed orders and customer complaints. Labels and a daily clear-out prevent this. [L1000.070]

### Post-sale calls, reviews and thank-you messages

**Post-sale review calls.**
- A customer service VA is well suited to make these calls, because support staff have quiet periods when no inbound calls come in. Calls can be made in these slots. [L1011.014] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- Post-purchase review calls are a simple, scripted task that VAs or customer service staff can handle during quiet periods. This makes them a good first task for a new VA, who can learn the customers and the market before taking live support calls. [L1012.011] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- A customer service VA usually needs only about an hour of free time per day to work through the post-sale call list. [L1011.024] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- If the founder makes the calls, each call takes about 10 minutes. With about one new customer per day, that means roughly one call per day, which fits easily into a daily to-do list. [L1011.018] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- Set a fixed time slot for review calls, daily or weekly, and treat it as a recurring task. Start as soon as possible, even if the calls feel uncomfortable at first. [L1012.024] (Source B, 2021-22, older)

**Delegating post-sale calls.** [L1011.015] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
1. Give the VA the call script and ask them to read it first.
2. Have a short conversation and answer any questions they have.
3. Walk them through the process step by step.
4. Instruct them to make the calls during downtime.
5. Each day, have them check the list of customers who have reached the call point, meaning about 10 days after purchase or after delivery, and call those customers.
6. Add the calls to the standard operating procedure for customer service.

**Implementation steps.** [L1011.019] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
1. Develop your call script.
2. Decide who makes the calls: yourself or a VA.
3. Add the calls to your daily to-do list, or to the SOPs and to-do lists of your employees or VA.

**Reviews and feedback.**
- When delivery volume justifies it, hire one full-time employee dedicated to calling delivered customers for reviews and testimonials. In early-stage growth phases with inconsistent order volume, use the downtime periods to call every delivered order for feedback, photos or videos, rather than leaving gaps in the follow-up. [L0286.041] (Source A)
- Assign a team member to read reviews of your product regularly on Amazon and on competitor sites, and record the recurring complaints and wishes. Do this alongside your own store reviews so the findings cover the whole market. [L1155.019] (Source B, 2021-22, older)

**Thank-you messages.**
- Personal thank-yous can be scaled in two ways: with tools that keep a personal touch, or by delegating to team members. Video and handwritten notes are both viable, and a business can use either or both, since customers receive them at different times. [L0406.008] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- Start with individually recorded videos for each order when volume is low. Switch to one pre-recorded video once order volume grows too high to record daily. Individual videos can be sent daily, twice a week, or weekly while the business is still small. [L0406.016] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- When a store gets many orders per day, recording an individual thank-you video for each customer can take about three hours a day. At that volume, switch to one pre-recorded video that is sent to every new customer. [L0406.031] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- Any team member can record thank-you videos, not only the owner. The video should come from a person in the business. A top customer service agent can record them in downtime and send them through Bonjoro with user access. [L0406.025] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
- If you send thank-yous personally, set calendar reminders or add them to your to-do list to keep consistent contact with new customers. If you delegate, document the process in the team member's standard operating procedures. [L0203.006] (Source B, 2021-22, older)

**Delegating the video thank-you.** [L0406.026] (Source B, 2021-22, older)
1. Run through the video thank-you process yourself a few times.
2. Write it down as an SOP, or fit it into your existing customer service or order-processing SOPs.
3. Give the task to a team member who has Bonjoro user access.
4. The team member logs in and sends the pre-recorded video to each new customer, which takes only a couple of minutes per day.

### Product uploads and freelancers

**Deciding to outsource uploads.**
- Product uploads are the most tedious part of store setup but must be done correctly. Checking the work of anyone you hire is essential. [L0855.165] (Source A, 2023)
- A supplier with bulk upload needs a CSV, which is a large spreadsheet. One mistake in one row or column can make many product descriptions or prices wrong. [L0855.166] (Source A, 2023)
- Product upload CSV work can be done by the owner in one evening (about four or five hours), following Shopify's step-by-step CSV import guide. If it fails, post the job with the CSV on Upwork with Shopify product upload experience. [L0856.101] (Source A, 2024)
- Use CSV uploads to add or update products in bulk. Shopify provides a template, and most product data can be managed in a spreadsheet before uploading. [L0665.106] (Source A, 2022, older)
- A supplier catalogue that already exists as a spreadsheet but is not formatted for Shopify can be uploaded by a freelancer on Upwork, Fiverr or Storetasker. Outsourcing saves time when a supplier has thousands of SKUs. [L0857.069] (Source A, 2025)
- Product upload effort depends on the supplier's data format. Ready-made Excel or CSV files upload quickly, while data spread over a supplier's website takes much longer per product. [L0667.053] (Source A, 2025)

**Freelance platforms and costs.**
- Outsourcing product uploads can be done on freelance platforms such as onlinejobs.ph or upwork.com, where workers can be hired at roughly $3 to $4 per hour. [L0663.040] (Source A, 2019, older)
- Experienced Shopify product upload freelancers on Upwork, often based in the Philippines, charge around $4 to $5 per hour. There is no fixed price per product, because it depends on how the supplier sends its data. [L0667.054] (Source A, 2025)
- Examples on Upwork were around $5 per hour for Shopify product upload freelancers (Source A, 2025). [L0807.050]
- On Fiverr, typical pricing for Shopify product upload services is around $10 per 50 products. Many freelancers work in different time zones, which enables overnight uploads while you continue supplier outreach. [L0128.005]
- Hire freelancers for bulk Shopify uploads through Upwork or onlinejobs.ph to build a consistent working relationship that can be reused as the supplier count grows. There is no fixed price; the cost depends on how much work the supplier data needs. [L1069.030] (Source A, 2019, older)
- For a full-time dedicated upload person, onlinejobs.ph was listed at $99 per month (Source A, 2019, older). [L0243.019]
- Image resizing has been quoted at about 10 USD for 100 images (Source B, 2021-22, older). The task needs no speaking or writing, so freelancers or VAs in any country can do it. [L1163.014]
- Fiverr and OnlineJobs.ph can be used to outsource upload tasks such as image resizing or writing product data at affordable rates. [L1163.015] (Source B, 2021-22, older)

**Hiring procedure, step by step.**

*Upwork with a recorded SOP (Source A, 2019, older):* [L0243.019]
1. Time how long you spend uploading a batch of 25 to 100 products.
2. Record your screen and voice using Loom, showing the complete upload process (20 to 30 seconds minimum).
3. Post the job on Upwork (free) with the Loom video as the SOP, or hire from OnlineJobs.ph ($99 per month) if you want dedicated full-time help.
4. Send the Loom SOP to the freelancer.
5. The freelancer handles the remaining bulk uploads while you focus on other tasks.

*Upwork with a test batch (Source A, 2025):* [L0667.052]
1. Record a screen and webcam video with Loom, a free tool, uploading 5 to 10 products from one supplier.
2. Narrate each step: get the product name, get the description, get the images, enter the price, and enter the SEO meta description.
3. Post a Shopify product upload job on Upwork, and send a test job of about 100 products to three or four applicants.
4. Hire the freelancer who does the test best, and give them the rest of the uploads.

*Upwork, filtered by track record (Source A, 2025):* [L0807.050]
1. Upload five to ten products yourself first, and confirm the process works.
2. Record the steps with Loom as a reference video.
3. Search Upwork for a freelancer with Shopify product upload experience.
4. Filter for job success rates of 90 percent or higher.
5. Send the recording and examples of finished products, so the freelancer can follow the same steps.

*Posting for free (Source A, 2023):* [L0855.163]
1. Post the job on Upwork for free.
2. Review each applicant's reviews and portfolio, asking for screenshots or video of previous work.
3. Hire, then double-check every upload row before publishing.

*Handing off across suppliers (Source A, 2021, older):* [L0671.082]
1. Do a few uploads yourself to learn the process.
2. Record your screen with Loom while uploading 10 to 20 products from one supplier.
3. Send the recording to whoever will do the uploads.
4. Treat each supplier as a separate job, because the work differs even when product counts are the same.

**Suppliers that give only basic data.**
- When uploading product lists from suppliers that give only UPC, SKU, name and price, upload three to five products yourself, record it, and have a freelancer do the rest. Spot-check the pricing afterwards. [L0852.106] (Source A, 2020, older)
- A store owner can outsource site design, product uploads and developer tasks to freelancers on Upwork. Record a screen video showing how you want a product uploaded, then have the freelancer upload 100 to 150 products. [L0852.105] (Source A, 2020, older)

**Scale and launch.**
- Launch traffic once around 30 to 50 products are live, and keep uploading the rest of the catalogue in parallel. Stores with over 3,000 products exist, and growth to that size happens gradually. [L0663.047] (Source A, 2019, older)
- There is no fixed maximum number of products. Keep uploading until every quality supplier in your niche is uploaded, then check regularly for new product lines and new suppliers. [L0835.124] (Source A, 2023)
- Each new supplier adds variety, and more products are better when they all come from suppliers in your niche. Adding suppliers over time is the way to grow the catalogue. [L0835.128] (Source A, 2023)

**VA platforms for uploads.**
- The same platform can hire VAs for product uploads too. Screen candidates, because a good VA can upload hundreds of SKUs per day. [L1274.077] (Source A, 2018, older)
- A store can scale with VAs for customer support and uploads. Full-time support staff can handle the work for an established brand, but a new store can start with a reliable VA platform. [L1274.079] (Source A, 2018, older)

### Catalog, content and pricing

**Product pages.**
- Maintain a consistent layout standard across all products. Ensure photo ratios are identical (for example, square) so they display properly in collections. Use a standard template for descriptions. Update the SOP template as you improve the design, and let team members contribute suggestions. [L0263.039] (Source A)
- Each new product added to a multi-product page requires the full cycle: duplicate, set Active, create its template, assign the template and save. Skipping any one of these steps causes content changes to affect other products. [L1013.017] (Source D, 2026)
- To audit products that are already live, work back through existing product pages and check each one against the on-page SEO checklist. This is simple enough to hand to a team member or VA, and most people find they are missing some items on older pages. [L1191.025] (Source B, 2021-22, older)

**Content and images.**
- Product content such as photos and videos usually comes from the supplier, so you rarely need to create it yourself. Free online tools are available for ad overlays and text on images or video. [L0358.054] (Source A, 2020, older)
- A VA can handle the repetitive work of uploading supplier product content and building product pages. Delegating these tasks frees your time for supplier relationships and strategy. [L0358.055] (Source A, 2020, older)
- Resizing large batches of images is repetitive, manual work that should be outsourced. [L1163.014] (Source B, 2021-22, older)

**Account location.**
- Upload from the team's own accounts and region rather than assigning posting to someone in another country, because account location affects reach. The owner had not yet considered this when outsourcing uploads. [L1077.035] (Source C)

**VA tasks in the store.**
- VA tasks in the store included handling pricing errors, updating products when suppliers change prices, and replacing product photos. One VA learned basic Shopify skills on the side to do these updates. [L0921.023] (Source A)
- Store speed fixes can be outsourced to Shopify experts found on storetasker.com or on Upwork. This is an option when the owner does not want to fix performance problems personally. [L1446.021] (Source A)

### Supplier outreach and product integration

**The integration cycle.**
- The product integration cycle is: gain supplier approval, upload their products immediately, return to supplier outreach, and repeat until all approved suppliers are live. [L0128.002] (Source A)
- Once a supplier approves you, upload all of its products immediately and remove the demo products. Continue contacting more suppliers on your list after the first approval. [L0835.127] (Source A, 2023)
- Upload new products from a supplier as soon as the supplier approves you, then check for new suppliers every six months. [L0665.109] (Source A, 2022, older)

**Recruiting suppliers.**
- For a new store, bring on 20 to 25 new suppliers per month. This creates a deep supplier pool to choose from when fulfilling orders. [L0263.036] (Source A)
- Track every supplier in a supplier tracking sheet with the date of your last contact, the outcome, and the next follow-up date. Use it to schedule follow-ups, such as contacting a rejected supplier again in three months. [L0835.126] (Source A, 2023)
- Supplier outreach, including contacting new suppliers, following up, and uploading their products, is a continuous task that never fully ends. Once it becomes too much for the owner, it is a candidate for a dedicated outreach person. [L1446.033] (Source A)

### Quality checks on delegated work

- Check every product upload closely, whether you do it yourself or someone else does. Wrong descriptions, measurements or prices from CSV or Excel uploads can go live unnoticed and hurt conversions. [L0470.033] (Source A, 2023)
- When outsourcing product uploads, check the work regularly. Common errors are the wrong image on a product or the wrong price on a product. [L0663.041] (Source A, 2019, older)
- Check that each product row matches its title, description and price before going live. [L1111.161] (Source A, 2020, older)
- If you outsource product uploads, work in small batches and double-check every price, image and product detail before publishing. Mixed-up prices or images are a common and costly error. [L0247.120] (Source A, 2021, older)
- Before a new VA uploads a full batch, ask for one sample upload per manufacturer and check it. This catches formatting or quality problems on a small scale before they affect the whole catalogue. [L0832.063] (Source A)
- Verify outsourced work before relying on it. Outsourced product review or approval work may give unsatisfactory results. [L1094.109] (Source A, 2023)
- Check the daily fulfilment routine closely in the first weeks, then reduce it to periodic review once the routine runs as planned. [L1358.029]

### Downtime tasks and customer insight

**Downtime ideas for a VA when the inbox is empty.** [L1000.044] (Source A)
1. Research suppliers that sell the same products, checking whether they offer better deals or have lowered prices.
2. Learn to upload products, so the assistant can handle new supplier listings when they are added.
3. If the business is mostly B2B, have the assistant look for potential customers and make cold calls to generate more sales.
4. Keep a shared task board, such as Trello, where the owner adds downtime tasks as they come to mind.

## Delegating content, creative and link building

### Deciding What to Delegate

- Write the first 10 to 15 articles yourself to confirm that content marketing works before hiring writers. Invest in a writing team only after the channel has proven results. [L0382.005]
- Additional content writing is not needed at the start. A few authoritative articles beat many generic ones, and outsourcing blog writing to Fiverr or iWriter is not recommended. (Older advice, 2019.) [L0357.061]
- Write product descriptions and SEO content yourself, or learn them from free training, before paying someone on Fiverr. The training covers SEO in detail. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0665.108]
- Build the business beyond your own hands by hiring people to replace your daily tasks step by step. Start with writing, the easiest role to delegate, then add email, design, editing and data entry. [L0380.016]
- Outsource tasks you cannot do well yourself, such as detailed mock-ups and graphics, once cash flow allows. Start with rough mock-ups yourself and bring in specialists to reduce errors as the business grows. [L1438.050]
- Decide between doing an edit yourself and paying a freelancer by comparing the hours a first attempt takes with the freelance price. Learning a new editing app can take days, so outsourcing simple tasks can cost less than the time they take you. [L1427.055]
- Outsourcing ad creative to freelancers does not guarantee strong results, and learning video editing from scratch takes a long time. Plan for both before relying on either. [L0341.024]
- Dropshipping content and product testing should be done by someone who can produce content rapidly, or by a freelancer paid to deliver within 24 hours. [L0577.065]
- Keep the creative team separate from routine operations. Copywriters need freedom to incubate, and their output drops when they are held to the same visible schedule as operational staff. [L1366.067]
- Creative and offer quality drive results more than campaign structure. A winning creative makes the strategy matter much less. Test many creatives and offers continuously, since creative testing is one of the largest parts of the job, whether done in-house or outsourced. [L0995.027]
- Dropshipping sites can use virtual assistants (VAs) for repetitive tasks such as social media, email, customer service and content curation. Use platforms such as AppWork (or alternatives such as OnlineJobsPH) to hire them, and focus your own time on strategy. [L0226.038]
- Create a clear role split for every task: the owner keeps strategy and final approval, and delegates the rest. [L0380.017, L0380.016]

### Hiring and Vetting

**Hiring the first content producer (older advice, 2021-22)**

1. Post a job for a freelancer, such as a writer or video editor. [L0381.013]
2. Give the candidate a test piece of work. [L0381.013]
3. Hire the best candidate. [L0381.013]
4. Start producing the first content piece with them. [L0381.013]
- Hire quickly and imperfectly rather than after a long search. The first producer can be replaced later once output starts. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0381.016]

**Hiring freelance writers on Upwork**

1. Write a job post that sets clear expectations: one article per week, a keyword with guidelines, and finishing the article with an editor. [L0382.007]
2. State the price: $50 to $100 for a 1,000-word article. [L0382.007]
3. Say you need to hire three freelancers, which attracts more applicants. [L0382.007]
4. Say that new Upwork freelancers without ratings are welcome, because you will vet them by reading their work. [L0382.007]
5. Expect roughly 20 to 40 applicants per post. [L0382.007]
6. Have an editor shortlist the top five applicants. [L0382.007]
7. Check each shortlisted candidate for a style match with your brand. [L0382.007]
8. Hire three to five writers and pay for a one-article trial. [L0382.007]
9. Hire the best trial writers for 10 more articles and keep only the best. [L0382.007]

- Freelancers abroad can disappear, get sick, or deliver work that does not match their samples. Always test a writer with a paid trial article before committing to more work. [L0382.008]
- Upwork gives access to writers and editors worldwide, and lower overhead can mean lower rates. Low rates do not automatically mean low quality, but a clear vetting process is needed to find strong candidates. [L0382.009]
- Writers from lower-cost markets can produce good results at the start. Ranking for bigger keywords requires native-level writing and higher pay, so pay more when competition for the keyword is higher. [L0382.010]
- Use a clear-writing guide, such as Paula LaRocque's book on writing, as the standard for judging editor candidates. Use it to recognise clear, natural prose before you hire an editor to screen writers. [L0382.059]

**Finding writers through industry communities (older advice, 2019)**

1. Join Facebook groups for your industry. [L0357.062]
2. Post a request for a paid article on a topic that matches the audience. [L0357.062]
3. Ask members with experience to message you privately. [L0357.062]
4. Hire a writer who already knows the product and the community. [L0357.062]

**Hiring a native English editor**

- Hire a native English editor you respect to review articles and emails written by non-native writers. Judge the editor by reading their own writing for clarity, conciseness and persuasion. [L0380.019]
- Savings only materialise when the editor works quickly. [L0380.019]
- A native editor needs about 15 minutes for a 500 to 600 word article, then does a second pass. If an editor spends more than an hour on an article that short, something in the process is wrong. [L0380.021]

**Hiring and vetting a content team member**

1. Post the role on freelance or job boards, such as Upwork, Fiverr or a Philippines-based virtual assistant job board. [L1077.033]
2. Interview candidates. For a video editor, consider a chat over WhatsApp with sample files. [L1077.033]
3. Have the new hire produce test TikToks. [L1077.033]
4. Audit those test videos for brand fit, message and compliance risk. [L1077.033]
5. If the work is good, give the hire posting access, or have a VA handle uploads. [L1077.033]

**Hiring the right role for the job**

- Written content needs a content writer. Video content needs an editor, plus a separate person to record if someone else is doing the recording. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0383.006]
- Higher-quality writers are less likely to do manual publishing tasks, so expect to hand posting to a separate virtual assistant. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0379.013]
- Name the producer for each piece, whether a writer, freelancer, VA or yourself. If several people work on one piece, such as a writer plus an infographic designer or a video editor hired on Fiverr, record each person so responsibilities stay clear. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0384.016]

### Pay, Test Projects and Briefing

**General rules for outsourcing content production (older advice, 2021-22)**

1. Always pay per project, never per hour. [L0379.005]
2. Always start with a test project. [L0379.005]
3. Use a quality job posting, with a template. [L0379.005]
4. Provide clear instructions to freelancers on what a piece of content should include. [L0379.005]
