  5. Schedule the ads to launch at 6 am Eastern time (or 8 am), so spend begins around 9 am after the 30 to 40 minute ramp. [L1436.050]
  6. Let the test run for the first day, then judge by CPC, CTR and sales. [L1436.050]
  7. Turn off ad groups that have spent the break-even amount without a sale when the landing page is performing well. [L1436.050]
- A winning product run through the TikTok scaling structure typically shows about 2 to 5 sales on day one and about 10 to 15 sales on day two. Results far below this suggest the test has not yet found a winner. [L1436.089]

### TikTok Aggressive CBO Scaling

- Aggressive CBO scaling on TikTok in order:
  1. Once a winning creative and audience are known, launch five CBO campaigns, each at $150 with four ad groups. [L1382.046]
  2. Use the same single winning creative and ad copy in each ad group, with open targeting: US, age 18+, all genders, no interests. [L1382.046]
  3. Use manual payment and add no more than $50 at a time. [L1382.046]
  4. After each campaign has spent about $50 total, check the results, and cut campaigns without a sale if any other campaign has one. [L1382.046]
  5. Cut any campaign whose CPC is above $1 and keep the one with the best CPC. [L1382.046]
  6. Scale the winning campaign after three profitable sales, first to $300 and then to $500 or more per day, with budget increases in steps. [L1382.046]
- Scaling structure for a winning product on TikTok:
  1. Take the winning creative (for example add 2), the winning ad copy and the winning audience (for example open targeting, US, 18+, all genders, no interest filters). [L1436.056]
  2. Launch five CBO campaigns at $150 each, each with four ad groups using the same audience and only the winning creative and ad copy. [L1436.056]
  3. Use manual payment and add no more than $50 at a time. [L1436.056]
  4. Let each campaign run until about $10 is spent, which is about $50 in total for the test. [L1436.056]
  5. Cut every campaign without a sale. If no campaign has a sale, cut any campaign with a CPC above $1 and keep the one with a CPC around $0.5 to $0.6. [L1436.056]
  6. When one campaign gives profitable sales (about three sales at 2x ROAS, with break-even ROAS around 1.4x to 1.5x), scale that campaign by increasing its budget step by step. [L1436.056]
  7. Check campaigns several times a day because cutting and scaling are needed continuously. [L1436.056]
- Source disagreement on the spend before the first check: one procedure says about $50 per campaign, the other says about $10 per campaign (about $50 in total for the test). Use the first check point that fits your campaign size. [L1382.046, L1436.056]
- Scale aggressively only when a campaign has shown several profitable sales, for example three sales at about 2x ROAS. Increase the budget in steps, such as $150 to $300, because TikTok tends to reward campaigns that bid more with better audiences. [L1436.058]
- Once a campaign has produced profitable sales, raise its budget in steps of $50, waiting between steps, rather than jumping straight to a large amount. Move from the starting $150 toward about $300 per day and later about $500 per day only as results hold. [L1436.090]
- TikTok scaling needs frequent hands-on checks during the day, because campaigns must be cut or raised soon after results show. Unlike Facebook, where an end-of-day review is often enough, plan to stay at the computer during scaling periods. [L1382.075]
- After a budget increase on a TikTok campaign with a strong creative, the platform tends to direct the campaign to better-bidding audiences, so performance can improve after a raise. Judge the result after some time rather than in the first minutes after the change. [L1382.076]

### Cutting Rules

- Stop any ad group or campaign that has spent its break-even CPA without a sale. Scaling requires this strict cutting rule. [L1436.060]
- A good campaign keeps producing sales at each break-even CPA spend interval. If it fails to sell over two break-even intervals, the chances it will sell later are very low. [L1436.061]

### TikTok Ad Timing

- Recommended TikTok ad times (Eastern time):

| Window | Use |
|---|---|
| 9 am to 11 am | Scaling time |
| 12 pm to 3 pm | Scaling time |
| 7 pm to 11 pm | Scaling time |

Ads often spend little overnight, when buyers are not in buying mode. [L1436.054]

- CBO sets the budget at campaign level, and the platform chooses which ad group receives the spend. Each ad group targets a different pocket of the large TikTok audience. [L1436.055]

### Protecting a Winning Product

- After three to five days of consistent sales, move to custom content to protect the product from copycats. This typically starts around day three. [L1436.064]
- Start with small, low-key influencer tests and grow the number of creators as sales come in. Small creators can help, and when many of them post the same product, larger creators may reach out to you. [L1379.048]

### Spark Ads and Coins

- Use Spark Ads for bigger budgets, roughly $100 to $500, and multiple ad groups, which can be created by copying an ad group up to 20 times. [L1429.047]
- Use coins for smaller budgets, such as $20 to $30, since coins count as one ad group. [L1429.047]
- Test organically first. Use coins only for a new product you are highly confident about or for a second account of a proven product, and move to Spark Ads once you have money to scale. [L1429.048]

### Duplicating Accounts for Proven Products

- After an account reaches about 10,000 followers and brings in sales, a second account promoting the same product can be created by someone else or on another phone. Reuse the videos that already performed on the first account. [L1429.025]
- Two accounts, each with about 10,000 followers and each getting about 1 million views per day, produce about 2 million views per day in total. Adding a link to each account multiplies the sales potential. [L1429.027]
- Duplicate accounts only for products that already perform. A good sign is strong sales per million views. If a product does not perform, copying its content is not worth the effort. [L1429.028]
- Assume competitors will copy winning videos and launch the same product. Competing against yourself with a second account captures more of the market before others saturate it. [L1429.029]
- Competitors can download posted videos and repost them without the watermark. Disabling downloads only partly limits copying, so plan for copies and keep ahead. [L1429.030]
- If a second account brings in thousands of dollars in sales, a third account can make sense if you can run and maintain it yourself. There is no fixed limit on accounts beyond your capacity to manage them. [L1429.031]
- A product account that grew slowly can still produce a big result after a later viral video. The cited example estimated that six million additional views could bring about 30,000 in sales. [L1429.032]

### Pinterest: Test Setup

- Test each new product at $5 per day, because Pinterest needs time to learn the audience and higher early budgets mostly waste money. [L1135.011]
- In the lesson's tests, products that were profitable at $5 per day were also the ones that stayed profitable, while a $100 per day budget did not improve results. [L1135.012]
- Start with a maximum of $5 to $10 per day for the initial test, and run three to five products in parallel if the budget allows, since one of them is likely to win. [L1135.013]
- Pinterest has no ad set budgets, only a campaign budget (CBO), so set the daily budget at campaign level. [L1135.029]
- Build the first test campaign:
  1. Choose the Conversions objective. [L1135.030]
  2. Name the campaign with the product name and the launch date. [L1135.030]
  3. Select the target country. US is the largest market; Germany and the Netherlands also work. [L1135.030]
  4. Set the campaign status to Active. [L1135.030]
  5. Set the daily budget to $5. [L1135.030]
  6. Select run on specific days and choose the next day as the start date. [L1135.030]
  7. Set the launch time to 12:00 AM UTC. [L1135.030]
- Pinterest uses only UTC for scheduling, with no local time option, so launch campaigns at midnight UTC. [L1135.031]
- To set up the next Pinterest test, duplicate a campaign that already works and change only the product name, launch date and targeting. Keeping the budget, country and schedule settings unchanged makes the new test easier to compare with the previous one. [L1134.038]

#### Pinterest Targeting and Settings

- Ad group level: name the ad group stacked interest, then under Targeting details choose the interest keywords option and select interests from the dropdown. [L1135.034]
- Select 5 to 20 interests, ideally 7 to 10 closely related to the product. Choose interests that describe the target audience rather than only the product itself. [L1135.035]
- Keep the total audience size between 5 and 10 million, which the lesson calls the sweet spot, and never above 30 million. [L1135.036]
- Select sub-interests individually instead of whole parent categories. The audience shrinks when sub-interests are added, but individual selections let you see which interest generates sales later. [L1135.037]
- Demographics and audience: select only female if the product is for women, and set the age range from 21 and up, or 25 and up if that fits the product. Location: US or specific countries. Leave language and devices unrestricted and keep the default budget and schedule. [L1135.038]
- Optimization and delivery: set Optimization to Checkout and Delivery to Automatic, then keep the ad group active. [L1135.039]

### Pinterest: Scaling

- Scaling a winning Pinterest campaign:
  1. If results are highly profitable after day 3, double the budget. [L1133.010]
  2. Find more interests and keywords and put them in a separate campaign. [L1133.010]
  3. Launch the new campaign at higher budgets. [L1133.010]
  4. Move to CPA-based scaling. [L1133.010]
- Source disagreement on the trigger: one procedure doubles the budget when results are highly profitable after day 3. The other requires the campaign to stay above breakeven for two consecutive days. Use the day 3 check for highly profitable results and the two-day check for a stable campaign. [L1133.010, L1135.054]
- Scale a campaign only when its ROAS stays above breakeven for two consecutive days. The lesson example is a breakeven ROAS of 1.5 and an actual ROAS of 2.5, after which the daily budget doubles from $5 to $10. [L1135.054]
- Raise budgets at the start of the day, around 12 AM UTC, or up to 4 to 5 AM UTC. Do not raise them at 6 PM or 10 PM, because a late increase spends within one to two hours. [L1135.055]
- Scale a winning product:
  1. Confirm the campaign has stayed above breakeven ROAS for two consecutive days. [L1135.056]
  2. Double the daily budget at the start of the UTC day, for example from $5 to $10. [L1135.056]
  3. Open the targeting breakdown and select interest to see which interests produce sales. [L1135.056]
  4. Identify the interests that generate profitable sales, such as skincare or makeup. [L1135.056]
  5. Create new campaigns that target the winning interests and keywords, rather than only increasing the existing budget. [L1135.056]
- Horizontal scaling means building more campaigns with profitable interests or keywords, instead of only increasing the budget of one campaign. [L1135.057]
- For keyword campaigns, add 50 to 80 relevant keywords, use the add-all-results option to check audience size, and keep the audience between 10 and 30 million. [L1135.058]
- Advanced CPA scaling is considered only after 10 to 15 days of consistent results. It sets a maximum cost per acquisition, for example under $20, and requires detailed setup beyond the basic test. [L1135.059]
- Example: if a Pinterest campaign shows profitable sales broken down by interest, for example skincare and makeup at a ROAS of about 4 to 5, launch a second campaign that targets only the strongest interest, such as skincare alone. [L1135.062]

### Platform Updates: TikTok Inbox and YouTube Shorts

- TikTok's inbox added games that can be played inside direct messages and labels for organizing conversations. The update commentary notes that Instagram messages are still seen faster than TikTok DMs, so Instagram is the stronger channel for reaching creators you work with. [L1504.011]
- On YouTube Shorts, the dislike button was removed and replaced with Not interested and Don't recommend channel options. The heart icon also replaced the thumbs-up as the like signal, so creators should not expect dislike feedback on Shorts. [L1504.023]

## Seasonal peaks, cash flow and capacity planning

### Decide how hard to push in your first peak

- Treat your first Q4 as a learning season. Test and build experience rather than scaling aggressively, because going all in during a first peak often leads to returns, cash shortages and disappointment. Experience one Q4 before scaling into it. [L0780.004]
- A short-term seasonal product builds skills for a long-term brand: testing creatives, judging what a good ad looks like, and knowing when to add customer service staff or a video editor. Its early cash flow can fund the first steps of that brand. [L0347.040]
- A seasonal store is not the best first store. Adjust daily budgets by season instead of spending heavily out of season, and let budget changes follow the season rather than a fixed schedule. [L0470.060]
- Use slow periods to build assets and creatives while saving cash rather than spending on campaigns. Plan the year's key sales events and metrics ahead of time. [L1225.080]
- Start spending on ads only after the product makes sales. Once profit comes in, reinvest part of it into ads while organic traffic keeps flowing, so losses on ads are cushioned. [L1376.015]

### Plan the calendar and launch timeline

- Find the product before Q4, because it can then be scaled through the peak season, including Black Friday. [L0950.067]
- Start Black Friday campaign planning and setup 2-3 weeks before the event. One month ahead is more comfortable; two weeks is tight but doable. Delaying beyond this reduces time for testing and email sequence delivery. [L0308.032]
- Start planning for Black Friday and Q4 early and allocate a large amount of time to preparation: creatives, offers, email flows, customer service and stock all need setting up beforehand. [L1363.052]
- Plan launches and email messaging before the peaks rather than expecting them to happen automatically. Winners can be scaled by adding more products and building a stronger brand over time, and high-revenue periods such as Black Friday and Cyber Monday are where large single-day numbers are typically earned. [L0335.026]
- Preparing for Black Friday is still possible in October if the store, suppliers and ads are in place. Expect a holiday lift in most niches in November and December. [L1111.165] *(Source A 2020, older advice.)*
- Raise ad budgets before the holiday season, because prices rise as competitors increase spending in the last weeks. Retargeting visitors who came in early is cheap and helps capture sales later. [L1085.083] *(Source A 2018, older advice.)*
- Plan holiday traffic early: send as many people to the site as possible before prices rise, then remarket to them cheaply during the peak days. [L1085.084] *(Source A 2018, older advice.)*

#### Q4 launch timeline procedure

1. Choose the launch date before contacting suppliers, and keep it fixed in your planning. [L1115.075]
2. Set Q4 promotions with five offers: Halloween, Black Friday with Thanksgiving, Cyber Monday, Christmas, and New Year. [L1115.075]
3. Check inventory levels for key products before the busiest shopping season, and reach out to suppliers only for established products with low stock. [L1115.075]
4. Plan supplier approvals to finish by early November, leaving time to upload approved brands before Black Friday. [L1115.075]

#### Holiday store preparation

- Setting up holiday emails, audiences and remarketing ads takes about one hour for an experienced operator and about five to six hours for a beginner. Reusable email templates reduce the time needed. [L0623.013]
- Preparing for Black Friday and similar seasonal events, in order:
  1. Make sure your warehouse holds enough stock for the expected demand. [L1347.025]
  2. Produce new creatives, for example through a photo shoot for the product. [L1347.025]
  3. Update the store before the event. [L1347.025]
  4. Staff customer support so that customer questions are answered immediately. [L1347.025]

### Prepare stock and suppliers

- Before scaling daily ad spend beyond 2x, confirm the supporting infrastructure is ready:
  - payment gateway processing capacity and uptime,
  - supplier inventory levels and fulfillment speed,
  - internal team capability to manage order volume and customer service.
  Aggressive scaling without these systems causes payment failures, shipping delays, and customer satisfaction collapse. [L0321.047]
- Q4 (October to December) is the peak sales season on Amazon, so check inventory well before it starts to avoid stockouts. Missing Q4 can cost a large share of the year's sales. [L1153.052]
- When a supplier shows 10 or more orders per day, contact the supplier at once to secure stock before Chinese New Year, because factories close around 1 February while warehouses keep working for a few days. [L0349.024]
- Plan scaling and logistics before the end of Q1, since e-commerce usually slows down until late March. Prepare fulfillment capacity and consider ordering stock before a planned push. [L1173.039]
- Reorder stock of the best-selling designs, since sales data shows which ones perform. Limit stock of weak sellers. [L1439.044]
- Scale a product through repeat seasons, with new photos and creatives prepared in advance for each season. Sales from a winter product can grow with the next peak season. [L1439.045]
- Check the supplier for Black Friday discounts only with a clear volume plan. Ask for a specific discount ahead of time. The lesson's speaker had not requested one and was unsure suppliers would give one, so treat this as an open question. [L1363.051]
- Managing thousands of SKUs alone makes inventory discrepancies between your website and the supplier's system easy to miss. Build a routine to check stock and notify customers when their item is affected. [L1472.058]
- Order stock in proportion to ad budget changes: when you raise the budget by 20%, order stock in roughly the same proportion. Increase the budget only when performance has been consistent. [L1217.034]
- Do not scale ads in the same month you place a new stock order or change ad parameters at that time. Test first, confirm consistency, and only then scale. [L1217.035]

### Prepare customer service capacity

- As soon as scaling gets profitable (even £200/day profit), begin preparing customer service capacity. Hire virtual assistants or enlist partners in advance. The customer service backlog is often the biggest bottleneck when revenue spikes. [L0308.023]
- Major mistake: not planning customer service before scaling. £76,000 revenue over the Black Friday weekend required 3 virtual assistants and 2 staff members (5 people) and still resulted in 5 days late on customer service responses. This nearly destroyed the business. Always hire virtual assistants before profit scales dramatically. [L0308.022]
- Both sources cite 76,000 for the Friday-to-Sunday revenue but in different currencies: £ in the first case and USD in the second. Each figure is kept as stated in its source. [L0308.022, L1363.045]

### Build the cash position before scaling

- Aggressive scaling needs an extra 5,000-10,000 USD of cash for creatives and scaling to 2x or 3x. Plan cash flow before spending, because e-commerce is a cash-flow driven business. [L0363.069]
- When business grows, ad spend and other growth costs rise before the money comes back, so aggressive growth can outrun the cash and finances available to fund it. Know your real financial position before pushing growth hard, otherwise an active business with rising revenue can still lose profitability. [L0889.001] *(Source B 2021-22, older advice.)*
- Before scaling, secure enough budget to keep ads running continuously. One store owner ran out of cash for ad spend on a winning product, was forced to pause for about two months, and sales did not return to the earlier level after restarting. [L1172.062]
- Cash flow can be managed in several ways: wait for payouts before ordering from suppliers, use a private supplier, or use credit cards in a pinch. At small scale a personal or simple business account can be enough, but it gets messy as orders grow. [L1469.043]

### Protect against payment holds

- Prepare for PayPal cash hold restrictions. PayPal may restrict withdrawals to £18,000 per month if large sums flood the account suddenly, which severely impacts cash flow. Have alternative payment processors or credit lines ready. [L0308.021]
- Payment holds that freeze cash flow are a typical recurring setback. Plan for them as normal events. [L1042.003]

### Set ad budget rules for peak events

- Before raising the budget of a Facebook ad set in Q4, the product needs both a good conversion rate and good ad results. Scaling a product that only has high clicks but no sales spends budget without payoff. [L0774.057]
- Seasonal demand drops can be handled by reducing ad budgets in regions where demand is weak and keeping ads running where demand is still strong, using geographic targeting. [L0801.159] *(Source A 2021, older advice.)*
- Budget changes during a short event differ by approach:
  - **Short high-demand event (e.g. Black Friday):** the budget can be raised several times in one day when an ad set shows good results, as long as results are checked. This is a short-term approach for the event and not a standard scaling rule. [L0646.065]
  - **Standard scaling:** increase ad budget only when performance has been consistent, in steps of about 20%, with stock ordered in roughly the same proportion. [L1217.034]
  - Do not combine an event push with a new stock order or a change to ad parameters in the same month. [L1217.035]

### Run the Black Friday budget procedure

#### Black Friday scaling procedure (from the lesson)

1. Run ads for Black Friday week at a discount, and announce a bigger discount for the day itself. [L1363.044]
2. On the day, raise the ad budget every four hours. [L1363.044]
3. At each check, compare the sales made in the last four hours and check whether ROAS is still strong. [L1363.044]
4. If ROAS holds, raise the budget again; if it falls, stop increasing. [L1363.044]
5. Use strong image ads with big offers (up to 60% off) and run them for Black Friday and Christmas. [L1363.044]

#### Lesson case: budget doubling

| Checkpoint (every four hours) | Daily ad budget (USD) |
|---|---|
| 1 | 400 |
| 2 | 800 |
| 3 | 1,600 |
| 4 | 3,200 |

- In the lesson's case, the daily ad budget was doubled every four hours. This reached roughly 3,000 USD of spend on a single ad, with a Friday to Sunday revenue of 76,000 USD and a stated 7% return. [L1363.045]
- Retargeting cannot be relied on for Black Friday. When retargeting got weaker, the lesson's team shifted to cold traffic and lookalike audiences. Email and SMS remain useful for pre-sale reminders and for announcing an extension of the sale to customers who missed it. [L1363.049]

### Plan the post-peak and off-season months

- January and February can be strong for dropshippers because many competitors run out of stock: cost per mille (CPM) is cheap and buyers still purchase. Do not ignore post-holiday months. [L1363.050]
- Use the low-demand months to build creatives, assets and cash reserves rather than to run heavy campaigns. [L1225.080]

### Plan for recurring setbacks

- Typical recurring setbacks in e-commerce include payment holds that freeze cash flow, sudden ad account bans, team members making mistakes, multi-day stretches of stagnant sales, and poor results that drain momentum. Plan for these as normal events. [L1042.003]

## Expanding: second stores, markets, inventory and brand

### Readiness before a second store

| Source | Signal | Applies to | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source A (2020, older) | About $50,000 monthly sales from Google Shopping with at least a 20% margin. Roughly $10,000 net monthly profit from the first store is a helpful signal too. | Stores whose first store is built on Google Shopping | L0551.058 |
| Source C | Consistent orders of about $20k in sales and a healthy profit margin. Revenue is not profit, so a store doing $60k in sales may still be less profitable than expected. | General threshold; the source does not tie it to a channel | L1017.018 |
| Source A (2019, older) | Tens of thousands of dollars a month in the main store before adding other channels such as Amazon or eBay. | Expansion to other sales channels, not a second store | L0357.057 |
| Source A (2023) | Consistent traffic and sales before cloning a store. | Cloning an existing store into a new niche | L1114.060 |

- Build a second store only after the first is consistently profitable, makes steady sales and produces enough profit to support the owner. Launching both at once can copy the first store's mistakes into the second before they are fixed. [L0623.023]
- Grow one niche store until it is maxed out before opening a second store, because growing an existing store is easier than building a new one. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0671.282]
- Do not open a second store while the first still has only half its suppliers approved, supplier product files not uploaded, or unclear Facebook ads results. This leads to split focus, so the first store must be stable before expanding. (Older advice, 2019.) [L0467.055]
- Do not open a second store until the first one produces consistent orders, around $20k in sales, and the profit margin is healthy. [L1017.018]
- Before opening store two, check the following. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0671.283]
  1. Are you approved with all gold and silver suppliers in your niche?
  2. Are all of their products uploaded to your store?
  3. Are your product pages optimized?
  4. Is your customer support, by live chat, email and phone, as good as it can be?
  5. Are your traffic sources diversified across Google, Facebook, Bing and SEO?
  6. Have you scaled your ad spend?
  7. Do you have email marketing flows for different scenarios?
  8. Do you run monthly promotions to your social audiences and email list?
  9. Do you create content and have a loyal following?
  10. Are you objectively better than your competitors, with a clearer unique selling proposition and a better buying experience?
- Do not open multiple stores at the full Shopify fee of $39 per month without being profitable. Keep a proven niche with a winning product, and open a second store to test a new product once the first store has enough data and a working pixel. [L1316.042]

### Case: launching two products in new stores

- Starting point: earlier tests on a Denmark market and a TikTok product cost about $2,000 in ads for $2,700 to $3,000 in sales, a small loss, and a TikTok product stopped after about $100 spend. [L0335.025]
- Two products were then found with the method. Each was launched in a new store with a new Shopify store, new Meta ad account and new pixel, created about two weeks before the sales were reported. [L0335.025]
- One store did about $1,790 in a single day, the other about $1,500. One product was scaled within three to four days to over $1,000 per day. [L0335.025]
- Spend was about $500 to $600 per day on one product and about $400 to $500 on the other, with a return of about 3x to 4x ROAS. Combined sales were about $3,000 to $5,000 per day. [L0335.025]

### Store structure: one niche per store and when to separate

- Prefer one store per niche. Do not spread the same niche across multiple stores unless you are niching up to a broader category. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0268.057]
- Keep a niche store focused. If its niche passed all tests, do not expand it with unrelated products. Sell other product types in related stores instead. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0671.284]
- Set up each new store as a separate business when its niche is unrelated. Separate stores keep finances, revenue and future sale options clean. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0963.146]
- Build an additional store for a related product only if buyers would not buy both products. Combine them into one store if one product is a replacement for the other. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0963.147]
- A niche store with one shared pixel lets you reuse the pixel and its data across several tested products over time. A single-product store can waste a pixel, while a niche store keeps the audience data useful for later products. For example, four products at about $300 each means roughly $1,200 of testing spend that still feeds the same pixel. [L1215.017]
- Scale a store by building more stores and growing the best ones together. Keep each store's finances and bank accounts separate so a sale of one network does not affect the others. (Older advice, 2020.) [L1111.163]
- Once a store is consistently profitable, do not close it. Keep it running and build the next store on top of the same business. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0803.072]
- A first store should be evergreen (sold year round), not seasonal. A second store can be built as a clone of the first with the same suppliers, using a new name, logo and colour scheme, once the first store works. [L0806.093]
- Cloning an existing store with a new niche can be done after the first store is stable, but not before it has consistent traffic and sales. Suppliers already approved can be reused for the new store. [L1114.060]

### Growing within a store

- Expand the product line gradually, launching one or two new products per month in the same niche rather than many at once. Each product can then be tested with its own ads and page. [L0871.083]
- Once a store has repeat customers, promote new product launches to them first. This raises average order value and improves return rates. Keep existing customers served well while new products are added. [L0871.084]
- Niching sideways means adding product variations or a related category when a niche has too few profitable products. Do this only after return on ad spend is strong in the existing range. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0664.067]
- After a product proves itself in a native-language store, scale it across the market and keep creatives fresh. Creatives fatigue over time, so copy and visuals need to be refreshed when sales drop. [L0660.042]
- Reducing product features can lower cost without hurting sales when customers do not value them. For example, testing 30 designs per book instead of 40 keeps costs similar if buyers do not notice the difference. [L1153.058]

### Domestic saturation and expansion sequence

- When year-on-year metrics show repeating revenue declines at similar percentages, your domestic market is saturating. [L0041.050]
- Diversify in this order. First, product line extensions and new customer segments. Second, additional marketing channels. Third, international expansion. [L0041.050]
- Reserve international expansion for later-stage growth, because it requires VAT compliance, import-export coordination and multi-region tax navigation, which add significant operational complexity. [L0041.050]
- Expand in stages. First succeed in the home market, then add neighbouring German-speaking markets such as Germany and Austria. The long-term goal is a seven-figure brand. [L0492.060]

### Multi-country setup

- Use one store for North America (US and Canada). Use a separate domain or store for the UK or other continents. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0268.058]
- Procedure for multi-country expansion (2025):
  1. Create separate campaigns by country (Alpha US, Beta US, Alpha UK, Beta UK).
  2. Include the same products in each campaign pair.
  3. Use geo-targeting to show each campaign only in its target country.
  4. Separate all retargeting ads by country (RT 30 days US, RT 30 days UK).
  5. This allows tracking spend, profitability and ROI per country and per product. [L0278.019]
- When a new product is launched, test it across the five largest target markets for about one week. Then concentrate budget on the countries that produce the most sales. In one reported case the US and Australia led, and Scandinavian countries were added after they also produced sales. [L0693.058]
- Facebook ads in a second language or market require a store in that language. One store cannot cover all markets, so local-language stores can capture other European markets. [L0773.053]
- Build a localized supplier presence in each target region, with a visible team in the countries where you sell. Every country, for example Northern Europe, the UK, Germany, Southern Europe, Israel and Thailand, is at a different stage and needs a localized approach. [L0780.034]
- Order processing and customer contact depend on the country being sold to and the market being served. A process that works in one market may need adjusting in another. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1499.001]

### Amazon FBA

- FBA scales without operational overhead. Whether 10 or 800 sales per day, Amazon handles fulfilment automatically. You do not need to manage supplier emails, pack orders or arrange shipping, so the model allows focus on marketing and inventory planning only. [L0227.038]
- The Amazon Pro seller account costs $40 per month. Do not subscribe until the product is ready to list. Activate it only when you are prepared to begin inventory and marketing. [L0227.039]
- Maintain inventory levels carefully. Stockouts cause lost rankings and sales momentum. Use supplier lead time to forecast reorder points. For example, a 6-week lead time requires ordering when inventory reaches 6 weeks of expected sales. [L0227.040]
- Do not expand to other sales channels such as Amazon or eBay until the main store is consistently doing tens of thousands of dollars a month, and only when forced by limits on the main channel. (Older advice, 2019.) [L0357.057]

### Building an email list and product ecosystem from Amazon customers

- Create a customer email list from all Amazon sales using a gift card or bonus incentive. Email this list to promote your subscription box, new products or complementary offers. [L0228.041]
- Examples from the same model:
  - Parents buying baby products can be offered a discount on a zero-to-two-year-old subscription box.
  - Women can be offered a weight loss product discount code multiple times: immediately after purchase, at 6 months and at 12 months. [L0228.041]
- Design your FBA product ecosystem to feed a subscription box business or affiliate revenue stream. [L0228.042]
- Worked example: resistance bands gift customers a $50 weight loss product gift card, costing $4 in bulk. If they redeem it for a $50-$100 purchase, the affiliate margin is $46-$96. If they buy the highest-tier $350 item, affiliate revenue is $346. This creates additional profit per customer beyond the initial product sale. [L0228.042]

### International scaling from Amazon

- Scale a successful Amazon product into international markets such as the UK, Japan and Germany. Lower competition makes them easier to enter, and a translator can localize the product pages. [L1153.053]
- Projection for international expansion: 30 sales per day in the UK plus 30 sales per day in Japan (10 sales per day of each of three sets) would produce roughly $500 profit per day, or about $15,000 extra net profit per month. [L1153.054]

### Private labelling, 3PL and bulk inventory

- Hybrid model: test products via dropshipping with minimal upfront cost. Once a product proves profitable, manufacture it in bulk with a supplier (for example on Alibaba), store inventory at a 3PL warehouse close to your target audience (for example in the United States), and sell from stock while continuing to dropship other products to find new winners. [L0280.027]
- Stocking inventory at a 3PL warehouse gives better profit margins than dropshipping and enables faster shipping. This increases customer loyalty and repeat purchases. Meanwhile, continue dropshipping other products to identify additional winners. [L0280.028]
- Case: a wood products seller tested various wooden items (cups, tables, chairs) via dropshipping. Once certain products proved profitable, he manufactured them in bulk through an Alibaba supplier, shipped inventory to a 3PL warehouse and sold from stock. Products held in inventory generated significantly higher sales and profit margins than dropshipped products. He continued dropshipping other wooden products to identify new bestsellers for future manufacturing. [L0280.029]
- Do not attempt private labelling without first proving consistent sales. Starting with 3PL or private labelling on untested products wastes capital. [L0324.036]
- If you operate a multi-product store, gradually private label all products, not just one. Suppliers often negotiate free or discounted private labelling on some products if the total order is large. [L0324.037]
- When private labelling, account for fulfilment centre storage fees. Facilities may charge retainer fees, per-unit fees or no fee, depending on order volume. A poor sales month can trigger unexpected storage charges. [L0324.038]
- When scaling from dropshipping to bulk inventory, maintain original shipping speeds. Do not sacrifice delivery speed for lower per-unit costs, because slower fulfilment causes customer complaints, chargebacks and trust erosion. [L0324.046]
- When a supplier requires a full inventory commitment for a new collection, offer a trial with a few weeks of drop shipping first. Stock inventory only if sales prove the demand and the opportunity is large. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0801.160]
- Pre-buying inventory from a supplier is only worth doing when you sell enough units that stock might run out. Otherwise it locks up cash for a discount. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0551.060]

### First local stock order benchmarks

- A lower benchmark for a first local stock order is about 10 orders per day sustained for a month. A higher benchmark is 50 to 100 orders per day for 10 consecutive days before bulk stocking. [L0780.044]
- Starting with a small stock of around 10 pieces keeps the risk limited. [L0780.044]

### Wholesale and retail

- When retailers start asking for sales or scan data, hire an executive-level person to manage wholesale before the process becomes overloaded. In the example, the wholesale hire used existing relationships to win a chain of about 160 stores, which then reordered four additional products within a day. [L0334.050]
- Retail partnerships let you sell large quantities to one buyer at once, such as a chain buying tens of thousands of sets for its stores. This raises total revenue and supports a higher revenue multiple at exit. [L1153.055]
- Use airports and specialty bookstores as retail channels for small, light and portable products. These suit buyers who travel or need last-minute gifts. [L1153.056]
- Large retailers choose suppliers on a quarterly buying cycle. If an existing supplier underperforms in a holiday quarter, a new supplier can take its place the next quarter. [L1153.082]
- Have stock, packaging and paperwork ready before the buying window opens. Missing it by one quarter pushes the entry back. [L1153.082]
- A retail chain may first test a product in its stores in one country and expand overseas only if early sales look decent. Treat a domestic retail launch as a test that can unlock international distribution. [L1153.083]
- Once the store has settled, check whether the team can handle B2B sales through a trade page and special pricing. B2B marketing through publications and trade ad space works best as a later scaling step, since conversion rates are lower. (2023.) [L1094.110]
- Evaluate ClickBank as a sales channel, especially for supplements. The lesson cites a supplement company that reported growth from about $4 million to about $10 million per year within a week of joining. Treat that figure as an unverified anecdote, not a benchmark. [L0334.053]

### Brand building

- The shift from short-lived dropshipping stores to brands means building a brand that lasts. Test many products over time, then scale the winners with branding and support. [L0871.085]
- Physical presence helps buyers see and try the product. A pop-up store was run in London one summer, and pop-ups in the UK and abroad were planned, with wholesale stockists creating physical points of contact in cities. [L1438.052]
- A clothing brand that works on a cycle can release a new collection roughly every two to three months, each with its own campaign and theme. This keeps the brand fresh even when some products are similar to earlier ones in different colourways. [L1438.056]
- Fund clinical trials for the main product when a patent exists, because for supplements patented, clinically proven products are rare. The brand funded three trials and expected data about a year later. [L0334.051]

### Team and processes across several stores

- Running two or more stores at once requires a team for order fulfilment and customer support, because one person's capacity fills up quickly. Keeping the store count low and focusing on one or two stores is easier to manage alone. [L1017.019]
- Keep order processing and supplier relationships separate for each store, because mixing them across stores quickly becomes messy. Doing so protects each store's supplier contacts and workflow. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0949.035]
- Creative, marketing, development and technical roles can be shared across several stores when the person has spare capacity. Capacity and available time decide how far a role can be shared. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0949.036]
- When running several stores, reuse the processes and systems built in one store for the others to avoid redoing work. Building them once and documenting them makes each new store faster to launch. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0949.037]
- Each new store built on the knowledge of an earlier one is a repeatable process. The operator applies the same playbook to new sites, and reusing proven marketing and store setup work reduces the learning curve for every new project. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1014.009]

## Affiliate, partner and referral programs

### Long-term approach

- Dream 100 outreach and affiliate marketing are long-term strategies. They succeed through small daily steps over months, not through intensive hours in single sessions. Consistency across all tasks matters more than the effort spent on any one day. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0202.009]

### Setting up an affiliate program

- Affiliate programs require proactive recruitment and management. Installing software and adding an affiliate link to your footer is not enough. You must actively identify and recruit potential affiliates. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0213.002]
- Do not install affiliate software and wait for affiliates to find your program. They will not discover it without direct outreach, so you must recruit them even though the infrastructure is in place. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0213.016]
- Setup procedure:
  1. Install affiliate software such as Refersion in your Shopify store.
  2. Identify your 10 bestselling, consistently in-stock products.
  3. Set commission rates for each product based on your margins (5-10% typical).
  4. Configure each product and its commission rate in the affiliate software.
  5. Proactively recruit affiliates and provide them with unique tracking links and dashboard login credentials.

  *(older, 2021-22)* [L0213.015]
- Affiliates need dashboard access to monitor their performance, including sales count, click-through rates and pending payouts. Without visibility into results, they cannot judge whether their promotion works and will disengage. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0213.014]
- Reversion is affiliate management software. It stores product details and information in a backend portal where affiliates can access everything when they log in. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.004]

### Finding and recruiting affiliates

- Identify potential affiliates by researching influential content creators in your niche: bloggers, YouTubers, podcasters and social media personalities with engaged audiences. Contact them directly with specific affiliate offers and commission details instead of expecting them to find a generic affiliate link. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0213.018]
- Affiliate links are identifiable by extra URL parameters or query strings appended to the end, unlike plain domain URLs. Links with these additions indicate affiliate promotion. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0212.002]
- Email is your primary contact target. For websites, check the Contact Us page. For social media users, try private messages. For YouTube creators, check the About tab for a business email. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0212.003]
- To find potential YouTube affiliates:
  1. Search YouTube for *best [product category]*, using the same approach as a Google search.
  2. For each result, go to the About tab of the channel.
  3. Look for a business email address in the About tab.

  *(older, 2021-22)* [L0212.004]
- Use the affiliate outreach email template provided in the lesson resources. Customise it by filling in your affiliate program details, business information and specific offers before sending it to prospects. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0212.009]
- If a potential affiliate agrees to join but has not yet signed up, create a follow-up trigger to remind them with a brief check-in message. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0212.007]

### Tracking affiliates

- When recording affiliates in your tracker, include the individual's name if you can identify it. Otherwise, record the site name. To find an individual, check the website's About Us page or, for blogs, look for a consistent author name across posts. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0212.001]
- Affiliate tracking spreadsheet columns: *(older, 2021-22)* [L0212.005]

  | Column | Content |
  |---|---|
  | Name | Person or site |
  | Site/Channel | URL |
  | Contact Details & Method | How you contact them |
  | Target Product Type | What they currently promote |
  | Contacted? | yes/no |
  | Outcome? | yes/no, became affiliate |
  | Comments | Optional |

- Record the products each potential affiliate already promotes in the Target Product Type column. This helps you tailor your outreach to their existing audience and content. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0212.006]
- Use the spreadsheet for two purposes: to avoid wasting time contacting people who declined, and to keep a roster of who has enrolled in your affiliate program. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0212.012]

### Affiliate support packages

- For each product offer, create an affiliate support package:
  1. Original high-resolution product images in a shared Google Drive folder, so affiliates can edit them for their websites.
  2. Detailed information about product features and benefits.
  3. Product videos from your supplier or self-produced, with clear directions on where to find them.
  4. The cookie window duration and affiliate commission amount for that specific offer.
  5. Multiple banner ad formats, so affiliates can choose between banners or custom content.

  *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.005]
- Audit each product page to confirm it contains original product images, feature and benefit descriptions, product videos with clear directions, cookie window and commission details, and banner ad options. If the product page includes all these elements, direct affiliates to it instead of assembling separate support materials. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.013]
- In high-ticket dropshipping, do not give affiliate partners free products to test. Products become classified as used goods and cannot be resold afterward. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.006]
- Educate each affiliate partner on the top-selling product features, key benefits and the specific problems the product solves. Affiliates rarely receive the actual product, so they cannot learn these points themselves. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.007]
- If an affiliate has already bought and used your product, they may create their own promotional content instead of using your standard materials. This is acceptable and can be valuable because it reflects firsthand experience. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.012]

### Managing and communicating with affiliates

- Actively manage and support affiliate partners after recruitment. Passive approaches fail because affiliates have many other products to promote. Support helps them succeed and stay engaged long-term. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.001]
- Affiliates maintain networks and communities where they share successful offers and partnership experiences. When you give strong support, partners refer others to your program by word of mouth without extra outreach. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.002]
- Klaviyo and Drip are email management platforms. Use them to maintain a dedicated communication list with all active affiliate partners. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.003]
- Expecting affiliates to research products and create all promotional content from scratch, without support, significantly reduces their success rate and engagement. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.011]
- Communicate with affiliate partners regularly whenever you launch new offers, change or update products, release new models, or make other business changes. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.008]
- Ask individual affiliates which products their audience is looking for, and invite them to suggest products for new offers. Do not promote only your pre-selected offerings. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.009]
- Regular communication and two-way engagement build lasting relationships. This improves retention and increases active promotion. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.010]
- Periodically check in with affiliates and ask whether you can help them with anything. Mirror your customer service approach instead of only pushing promotional opportunities. Combine this with regular product updates and new offer announcements. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0211.014]

### Dream 100 outreach

- Do not contact all 100 partners on day one. Reach out in batches over time, follow up with non-responders every week or every two weeks, and dedicate time to the partners who respond. [L0528.042]
- Without logging the contact date, the response and the next follow-up in the sheet, outreach gets forgotten and responses are lost. Every contacted partner needs a date and notes. [L0528.043]
- Dream 100 outreach is a recurring activity for the lifetime of the business, not a one-time setup. Put it in the calendar for one hour a week or one hour every two weeks. [L0528.044]
- Working-sheet procedure:
  1. Email the partner's contact address with specific collaboration ideas.
  2. When a partner responds positively, copy their row from the master list into the working sheet.
  3. Enter the date you contacted them.
  4. Write a note on the potential collaboration, such as a podcast appearance or a guest post.
  5. Follow up on any partner who has not replied, using the same sheet.

  [L0528.045]
- Dream 100 relationship work can only be done by the business owner. Comments, feedback and conversations written on your behalf lose their value when someone else writes them. A virtual assistant can still handle the research phase, for example by compiling YouTube channels above a set subscriber count on a given topic, if given clear instructions. *(older, 2021-22)* [L0529.003]

### Referral programs

- An automated referral flow is built once and then runs indefinitely in the background without ongoing manual work. It scales without adding staff time. [L0417.024]
- A referral program can pay affiliate commissions to members who refer others. The referral links are tied to member logins. *(older, 2021)* [L0834.106]
- Members refer others for three core reasons: they know people seeking financial freedom or a real business skill, they want someone to compete and build alongside, and they want peers in their community. [L0214.007]
- When recruiting referrals, show results and evidence that the program works, such as wins and earnings examples, rather than making promises. Combine this with your personal testimony, because an existing member's authentic endorsement carries more weight than marketing materials. [L0214.010]
- Referral procedure for E-Converts:
  1. Show the prospect results from inside E-Converts: pull up the wins channel, share earnings examples (16-year-olds earning $20,000+ monthly, 17-year-olds earning $500,000 in 90 days, adults earning six figures monthly), and share your personal experience. Members' endorsements are more convincing than marketing materials.
  2. Send them the referral link and direct them to book a call with the team, instructing them to enter your Discord name in the *Did someone recommend you?* field during booking.
  3. Once they join and complete their payment, you receive your 25% commission within 48 hours.

  [L0214.005]
- Commission rates depend on the program. Product affiliate commissions are set per product on your margins, typically 5-10% *(older, 2021-22)* [L0213.015]. The E-Converts member referral commission is 25% [L0214.005].
- Reinvest affiliate commission earnings into the business. Allocate the money to testing new products, scaling advertising or other growth activities, so referral income becomes leverage for your own scaling. [L0214.011]
- You can refer anyone to E-Converts: a friend, family member, co-worker, or anyone in your direct messages whom you believe would benefit from the community and succeed in it. [L0214.012]

## Business partners, mentors and community support

### Deciding whether to take a business partner

- Avoid business partnerships with friends or family members. The exception is a family member who performs specialized contracted services, such as graphic design or web design, on a project basis. That is acceptable. [L0276.052]
- Partnerships are hard to keep working. Write a contract before any work starts that defines each partner's responsibilities, and include clauses for buyout or removal if a partner does not deliver. *Older (2021).* [L1279.083]
- Avoid managing partners and equity partnerships early. Roles and effort often become unbalanced once a store takes off, and partners can fall out. Build the store from zero to one with your own effort first, then outsource the day-to-day work. *Older (2021).* [L1090.135]
- The partnership model can be repeated several times with different people, creating multiple extra income streams. Overseeing 5 to 10 or more stores this way avoids working 8 to 12 hours a day running each one yourself. [L1125.005]
- Sources differ in emphasis. The caution against early equity partnerships applies to your first store. The repeatable partnership model applies when you expand beyond one store. [L1090.135, L1125.005]
- Partnering to run several stores at once is a higher-risk expansion than building one store alone. It offers a larger payoff if it succeeds, but the outcome is uncertain. [L0826.020]
- Two people working as a team who find synergy tend to perform very well and move faster than solo operators. [L0780.013]
- Choose business partners, suppliers and collaborators as deliberately as you would choose close friends. They shape the quality of your daily work and the time you have left for it. [L1343.042]

### Choosing partners

- When choosing partners to run stores, prioritize people who have completed proper training, have already made a first sale, and are fully committed. If the program requires it, also check that they are willing to relocate to the operating location. [L0826.019]
- Surround yourself with people who are more talented than you in their areas, since your output tends to match the quality of the people and inputs around you. If you are the most talented person in your group, look for a group where you can learn more. [L1343.022]

### Defining roles and decision rights

- Define each partner's role in writing so that responsibilities do not overlap or fall through the gaps. [L0829.004]
- Define roles with this procedure:
  1. List what each partner is good at and what each is not good at.
  2. Assign ownership accordingly.
  3. Use personality type, such as introvert or extrovert, to decide who handles outreach and who handles ads and back-end work. [L0832.009]
- Give each partner their own domain and the freedom to decide within it, so they do not need to approve every small choice. Escalate only decisions that affect the shared plan or money. [L0832.010]
- Partners do not need to report every routine action, such as a refund, to each other. Escalate only significant decisions and changes. [L0832.016]
- Partners in a small business should be willing to take on tasks outside their formal role when needed. [L0829.015]
- Share the workload and the stress of the business equally between partners instead of letting one person carry it. [L0829.021]
- Schedule important business conversations at a set time on a workday, not in social or family settings. Before the conversation, prepare the proposed solution, the reasons for it, and its pros and cons so a decision can be made quickly. [L0832.015]

### Working with family members

- Give family members clear rules and a written job description before they start working, the same as any other hire. Family work benefits from explicit expectations. [L0832.054]
- Working with family can work well, but set the rules first and treat the arrangement as a business relationship. Avoid vague arrangements that make it hard to ask for results. [L0832.055]
- The exception for contracted specialized services is covered under "Deciding whether to take a business partner." [L0276.052]

### Coaching and mentor programs

- Growth in dropshipping depends on three resources: a coach, virtual assistants, and continuous education. Plan for all three as the business scales. [L0345.005]
- An outside coach who audits the whole store and delivers a prioritized task list can produce a quick sales lift. In one case, nearly $6,000 more in monthly sales came after the first coaching call. [L1437.021]
- A coach's advice is that learning is best when you focus on one approach and avoid switching between too many tools. Once a process works, keep it. [L0964.154]

**Mentor programs (Source A)**

- Join the Mentor Program by logging into the members area, clicking "Get Help," then selecting "Private Mentorship." No application is required. Book weekly 30-minute one-on-one video calls with mentors who earn a minimum of $100,000 per month. *Older (2022).* [L0249.081]
- Choose mentors by specialty: paid traffic (Google Shopping, Facebook), conversion optimization, supplier relationships, exclusivity negotiation, pricing terms, or web design. Select based on your specific needs that week. *Older (2022).* [L0249.082]
- A weekly 30-minute accountability call with the mentor is worth it even when there is little to discuss, because it keeps progress on track. Pooling unused call credits across months is not practical with current booking systems. *Older (2022).* [L0429.034]

**Monthly coaching (Source B)**

- Monthly coaching accountability includes defining specific action items for the next 30 days and reviewing completion status, obstacles, and reasons for incomplete items at the next call. *Older (2021-2022).* [L0035.007]
- In the Master Plan 12-month structure, each client selects when to schedule their monthly coaching session within the upcoming month, rather than being assigned fixed dates. *Older (2021-2022).* [L0035.009]
- Members can work with different coaches from the Dropship Breakthrough team for individual sessions, including founder John, coach Ben, and other available coaches. *Older (2021-2022).* [L0035.010]

**Source C mentoring**

- Mentors usually answer student questions within one hour, or within 48 hours when they are traveling. Send questions in advance through a form before group calls so the mentor can prepare answers. [L1017.010]
- One call per week is the standard during the build phase, and calls can be spaced further apart depending on progress. Several calls per week are only useful in the scaling phase, when ads are live, sales are coming in, and Facebook campaign decisions are needed. [L1017.011]

**Source D support structure**

- Effective program support combines three layers: a personal pod with a coach and a Client Success Manager, a shared community channel for general questions, and scheduled live Q&A calls. Together they make sure a participant always has a clear route to get help. [L1482.001]
- In a personal pod, each participant gets a dedicated coach with real operating experience at six-figure revenue, plus a Client Success Manager (CSM) whose job is to keep the participant on track with their plan. The CSM role is about follow-through and accountability, not only answering questions. [L1482.002]
- A weekly live group call is a working session where students bring their numbers, ads, product pages and stuck points, and the coach reviews them in real time. The purpose is to unblock students each week, not to sell. [L1452.008]
- Combining one-on-one guidance, group knowledge and live call support gives you both personalized answers for your business and the benefit of collective experience. This combination shows you the next concrete step to take. [L1482.010]
- Revenue-based tiers in the Source D community. Members move up when they hit the next milestone, and each move assigns them a coach who specializes in that revenue stage. [L1323.015]

| Tier | Threshold to reach it |
|---|---|
| Beginner | Just starting, no sales yet |
| First sale achieved | First sale completed |
| $500/Day Club | $500 per day in sales |
| $5k/Month Club | $5,000 per month in sales |
| $10k/Month Club | $10,000 per month in sales |
| $30k/Month Club | $30,000 per month in sales |
| $100k+/Month Elite Builders Club | $100,000+ per month in sales |

[L1323.015]

- Do not hold back questions because you fear they are too basic or that you will speak up in a group. Asking early is what gets problems solved, and the team can only help with struggles that are reported. [L1482.008]
- Consuming all the material without asking questions hides real problems, because the support team cannot see that you are stuck. Raise struggles in the pod, the community or the calls as soon as they appear. [L1482.009]

### Booking calls

**Booking a one-to-one coaching call (Source B)**

*Older (2021-2022).* Follow these steps:

1. Open the booking link for coaching sessions. It is the same link for every call, and it is also sent in your confirmation email.
2. Look at the calendar. Days with available slots are highlighted in blue. Click one.
3. Check the time zone shown above the times. It is detected automatically; if you will be in another time zone, open the time zone dropdown and choose a different one.
4. In the panel to the right of the calendar, select a time slot.
5. Click the blue Confirm button.
6. Enter your Name and Email. Both are required.
7. Optional: write something in the field "Please share anything that will help prepare for our meeting."
8. Optional: enter your mobile number to receive SMS reminders. The country code is preselected based on your location.
9. Click Schedule Event.

[L0362.001]

- The one-to-one booking link is listed in the resources section below the course video and is also sent in the booking confirmation email. The same link is used for every one-to-one call, so it does not need to be requested again. [L0362.011]
- A booked coaching call lasts one hour and is listed as "1 hr" on the booking page. [L0362.002]
- Coaching calls are held on Zoom. The Zoom link is provided only after booking is confirmed and is sent in the meeting confirmation email. [L0362.003]
- Available slots are offered in 30-minute increments. Every slot shown can be booked directly, with no need to ask the coach beforehand. [L0362.004]
- The booking page is connected to all of the coach's calendars, so only times the coach has marked as available are shown. If an expected time does not appear, it is already booked and cannot be selected. [L0362.005]
- Time zone: the booking page detects your time zone automatically and shows times in it. To change it, use the time zone dropdown, which has a search field. This is useful when traveling or when booking for a future time zone. The dropdown also offers an am/pm or 24h time format toggle. [L0362.006]
- Name and email are mandatory on the booking form. The email address is needed to send the meeting confirmation with the call link, and the booking cannot be completed without it. [L0362.007]
- The preparation field is optional. A pre-coaching survey is sent afterward, asking which topics to cover and what to share about the business, so the free-text field does not have to be filled in. [L0362.008]
- SMS reminders are optional. Enter the mobile number in the "Send text messages to" field. The country code loads automatically based on your location and can be changed. [L0362.009]
- The form has an optional "Add Guests" field below the preparation box. It is not required to complete the booking. [L0362.012]
- After Schedule Event, the call is added to the coach's calendar and to your own calendar. The confirmation email then contains the booking link and all details needed to join at the booked time. [L0362.010]
- After booking is confirmed, the confirmation page tells you to join the call with the Zoom link from your booking confirmation or calendar entry. The page also shows the booked time with the time zone name, so you can check it before the call. [L0362.013]

**Booking mentor and coaching calls (Source A)**

- Book your own coaching calls through the members area: go to Blueprint, hover over "Activate," click "Activate Coaching," then select available times. You choose when to schedule; calls are not pre-assigned by the company. *Older (2022).* [L0272.070]
- Call lengths differ by program. Source B's one-to-one coaching calls last one hour [L0362.002]. Source A's mentor calls are 30 minutes each [L0249.081]. Check the length shown when you book.

### Peer networks and masterminds

- Work with your community instead of alone. Talk to others in the same position, learn from their mistakes, and elevate together. Solo work is significantly harder and less effective. [L0037.009]
- From the start, talk with peers about campaigns and goals through Google Ads support, Quora, Facebook groups, private forums and masterminds. [L0463.017]
- Get advice from other ecommerce entrepreneurs without needing to share your store. Peer networks help you learn the same tools and logic and can lead to partnerships. [L0780.014]
- A mastermind group should be a small private group of 2 to 5 people, ideally owners who each spend less than $1,000 per month on Google Ads. Members share information and work together on scaling profitable campaigns. [L0463.018]
- Online marketing is competitive. A supportive group of peers makes the workload feel easier and helps keep momentum during scaling. [L0463.019]
- Joining a community of established store owners is a practical way to get a second set of eyes on a store, such as a peer review of the homepage and policy pages. [L0855.171]
- Brand ambassador community: build it through a private Facebook group, with reasons to join such as brand appeal, a social cause, and rewards. Aim to grow it to about 20,000 members, with about 3,000 reached by the time of the lesson. [L0790.085]

### Community resources and membership rules

**Discord and live calls**

- Use Discord community channels and monthly coaching calls to stay engaged, ask questions, and maintain accountability while executing your scaling plan. [L0195.004]
- Source A live group coaching calls are held on the first Wednesday of each month at 2 PM EST, hosted on Discord. Attendance is included in the membership as a bonus. [L1070.001]
- All live coaching calls are recorded. Replays of the previous three months are available, so missed calls can be watched within that window. [L1070.002]
- Source D live Q&A calls take place every Thursday at 8 p.m. Singapore Time (SGT) in the Voice channel of the Discord community. [L1482.006]
- Weekly Source D Q&A calls cover common roadblocks, advanced strategies to scale faster, and live reviews of stores, ads and funnels. You can ask anything in real time and receive direct feedback from the mentors. [L1482.007]
- Sources differ on schedule because they cover different programs. Source A's live calls run monthly on the first Wednesday [L1070.001]. Source D Q&A runs weekly on Thursdays [L1482.006]. Follow the schedule of the program you belong to.

**Where to ask what**

- Source D uses Discord as the community hub. The general community channel holds shared insights, updates and wins, and the Voice channel hosts the live Q&A calls. If the general channel is not visible after joining, report it to support right away. [L1482.005]
- Take personal and detailed questions about your own business to your small pod group, because that is where you receive feedback specific to your situation. [L1482.003, L1482.011]
- Post broad questions, such as what is currently working in ads or tips for choosing suppliers, in the main community channel. There you can learn from the combined experience of many operators, not only from the coach. [L1482.004, L1482.012]
- The shared community draws on the experiences of more than 100 other operators, so you can learn from many peers and not only from your assigned coach. This is most useful for broad, current questions. [L1482.012]
- Use the chat to share community wins rather than reposting questions. Conversations that include wins are encouraged in the share-your-wins channel. [L0964.151]
- Check Discord channel retention times in the Welcome channel. The social and for-sale channels clear their posts, while the module channels are kept. [L0964.152]
- Use the members area Bonuses section to find past coaching call replays and the links mentioned in them. [L0964.153]

**100K club eligibility**

- Shopify's Discord-based 100K club is open only to stores that have already done more than $100,000 in sales. Store owners below that threshold should not expect access yet. [L0430.001]
- The 100K club is a peer group on the course's Discord. Stores can apply to join once they have done more than $100,000 in sales. Eligibility is based on sales volume, not on time in business. [L1093.009]
- Member community rule: the 100K community is for store owners who have done at least $100,000 in sales. Use it for peer questions alongside coach answers. [L0856.108]
- Sources differ on the threshold wording. Two units say "more than $100,000" [L0430.001, L1093.009], and one says "at least $100,000" [L0856.108]. Confirm the current rule with the community before applying if your sales are exactly at $100,000.

**Onboarding (Source D)**

New member onboarding in an online ecommerce community:

1. Create your Shopify store first, using the trial link provided in the community.
2. Join the Discord server and open a private support ticket. This is where support, coaching and progress tracking happen.
3. In the ticket, introduce yourself with your name, your goals, and whether you are completely new or already experienced, so the right helper is assigned.
4. Send a short greeting in the welcome channel to unlock the other channels.
5. Open the course and start with the first module, then work through the modules in order.

[L1323.017]

## Operating rhythm: tracking, finances and change logs

### Daily and weekly checks for a live store

#### Daily and weekly routines

Older advice from 2020:

Daily checks for a live store:
1. Confirm that all orders are going out. [L0961.081]
2. Keep the inbox at zero. [L0961.081]
3. Return every missed phone call. [L0961.081]

Weekly checks for a live store:
1. Audit every ad account to confirm that spending is profitable. [L0961.082]
2. Optimize any campaign that no longer makes money. [L0961.082]
3. Put more budget behind products that are earning well. [L0961.082]

Older advice from 2021: daily work after launch should include contacting new brands, following up with brands already contacted, and uploading and optimizing products, not just running ads. [L0962.133]

#### Weekly supplier outreach

- Schedule supplier outreach on a fixed weekday, ideally Monday to Friday, because many suppliers are closed on weekends. If Monday does not suit you, pick any weekday and block it in your calendar. [L0938.008]
- Set a recurring weekly calendar reminder for supplier outreach. Plan for the next one to two months, then keep the routine going as long as you are still building your supplier list. [L0938.009]
- Allow 30 minutes to 2 hours per week for supplier outreach, done as one dedicated block on a single day. Consistency matters more than the length of any single session. [L0938.010]

#### Daily reporting on new product tests (Source D 2026)

Daily reporting sequence for a new product test:
1. After the store or website is built, send the first report within 24 hours. [L1355.002]
2. After the product videos (creatives) are found, send the report within 24 hours. [L1355.002]
3. After the campaign is launched, report at the 24-hour mark for Day 1. [L1355.002]
4. Report within 24 hours after Day 2. [L1355.002]
5. Report within 24 hours after Day 3. [L1355.002]
6. Continue reporting every 24 hours after that until told otherwise. [L1355.002]

- Missing a daily report costs money and removes the support team's control over the test, so all required reports must be submitted before moving on to the next step. Do not proceed to a later phase while earlier reports are outstanding. [L1355.006]

### Tracking setup

- Inventory tracking is commonly disabled even for live stores because continuously checking stock across all products is impractical. Enable it only if you maintain an active inventory management system. [L0036.030]
- An automation that writes the recovery date when a paid order comes in captures sales made when nobody on the team could call, such as at night or during time off. This keeps the sheet accurate without someone updating it manually. [L0737.012]
- Set up the sheet and automations early, even when one person handles everything, so the data is available for month-over-month improvement. Tracking is worth doing from the start rather than after the team grows. [L0737.017]

### Planning that feeds daily work

- Older advice from 2021-22: turn each priority into the full list of tasks needed to achieve it within six months, and mark recurring tasks as weekly so they are entered once. These task lists then feed the daily to-do list, and the 90-day rocks are derived from them. [L0447.016]
- Older advice from 2021-22: the priority task lists in the planner feed the daily to-do lists for the owner and the team, so one plan aligns everyone on the same top priority each day. [L0447.026]

### Changelogs

#### Why every change is logged

- Keep a running changelog of every change made to the store and its marketing, because over months you forget what was tweaked and why sales moved. Without this record, teams repeat failed tests and cannot tell which change caused a rise or drop in sales. Review it regularly so past experiments stay available when a similar idea comes up again. [L0410.001]
- Record every platform change in a log together with the date, the platform, the exact change, the expected result, and a baseline measured before the change. Fill in the result and its date later so each change can be judged on its own. [L1509.002]
- Compare results against a 90-day trailing baseline rather than a single day, because one day of data is too noisy to show whether a change worked. Write the baseline into the log next to the change so the comparison is fixed in advance. [L1509.003]
- A change logged without a baseline or a numeric expected result cannot be evaluated later. Always define the target metric and its current value before making the change. [L1509.004]
- Keep a dedicated record of every ad, supplier and store change so you can explain what moved the metrics. (Older advice from 2022.) [L0803.093]
- Keep a file of every letter, variant and result, because the record is what lets you repeat winners and avoid losers. A seller who loses the file must rebuild its knowledge from scratch. [L1369.108]

#### Setting up the changelog spreadsheet

Procedure:
1. Open the master Google Docs changelog template from the provided link; it is read-only, so click File and then Make a copy to get an editable version in your own Google Drive. [L0410.002]
2. Alternatively, use File and Download to save it as Excel or CSV and open it in any spreadsheet software. [L0410.002]
3. For each change, fill the columns: Date (date the change was implemented), Platform (where the change was made, e.g. Google Ads or Shopify Email), Change (what was changed), Expected Results (a hypothesis of the outcome, stated as a number where possible), Additional Notes (anything you will need to remember later, such as starting budget or baseline). [L0410.002]
4. When the experiment ends, enter the Result Date (the end date of the test) and the Results (what actually happened). [L0410.002]
5. Compare the result with the expected result, then decide whether to stop the change or to adjust it and log the adjusted version as a new experiment row. [L0410.002]

Changelog template column layout (Google Docs / spreadsheet):

| Column | Name | Content |
|---|---|---|
| A | Date | Date the change was implemented |
| B | Platform | Platform where the change was made (e.g. Google Ads, Shopify Email) |
| C | Change | Description of the change |
| D | Expected Results | Hypothesis of the outcome, e.g. a target ROAS or open-rate increase |
| E | Additional Notes | Anything relevant to remember later, e.g. starting budget, baseline figure |
| F | Result Date | End date of the experiment (e.g. two weeks after the start date) |
| G | Results | What actually happened, entered as a number |

[L0410.003]

#### Log structure and example entries

Log structure (one row per change, dates as Excel serial numbers):

| Date | Platform | Change | Expected Result | Additional Notes | Result Date | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 45733.0 | Shopify Email | Opt-in bribe changed from % off to quiz | Increase revenue per opt-in | 90 day trailing revenue per opt-in = $80 | | |
| 45701.0 | Meta Ads | Updated creative for remarketing ads | Increase CTR | 90 day trailing CTR = 5% | | |
| 45691.0 | Meta Ads | Launched Advantage+ Shopping Campaign | >10x ROAS | Starting budget of $20/day | | |
| 45660.0 | Google Ads | Launched Demand-Gen Remarketing Campaign | >10x ROAS | Starting budget of $10/day | | |
| 45653.0 | Instagram | 3x weekly posts using AI-generated images & copy | 5x monthly engagement on organic posts | Current engagement = 100/month | | |
| 45630.0 | Shopify | Offer extended warranties | Sell 5 extended warranties/month | NA | | |
| 45611.0 | Shopify Email | Replaced email profile picture with moving.gif | Increase open rates by 5% | 90 day trailing open rate = 45% | | |
| 45553.0 | Google Ads | Launched YouTube Remarketing Ads | >10x ROAS | Starting budget of $10/day | | |

[L1509.001]

Example of a Google Ads test logged in the changelog: on 18 September, YouTube remarketing ads were launched on Google Ads for the first time. Expected result: a return on ad spend above 10X, meaning more than $10 back per $1 spent. Additional note: starting budget of $10 per day. Result Date: two weeks after the start.
